CL test. The reduction follows the architecture of~\cite[Section 7]{JNVWY20};
the canonical-line soundness theorem of~\cite{JNVWY21qld} is related background.
Lemma~\ref{lem:ms-direct-anticomm} supplies the base anticommutation directly.

\paragraph{Comments.} This is a substantial remaining formalization target and the
workhorse of question reduction: introspection certifies the players' sampling of
their own questions against Pauli basis measurements. The reduction uses direct
Magic Square anticommutation, linearity and orthonormalization, together with the
seeded CL theorem. The available canonical-line theorem can replace the paper's
classical route through Remark~\ref{rem:lidt-cl-adapter}'s adapter, which supplies the
$\mathrm{ldc}=1$ case this reduction uses (Theorem~\ref{thm:lidt-cl-soundness-one}).

The theorem's bipartite statement matches the pipeline's $\valstar$ soundness
interface, so its uses require no additional synchronous-value transport. The verification
campaign~\cite{Audit26} further reports three corrections in the tensor-code material the
proof passes through --- $\kappa$-convexity requires $c_1 \leq 1$, the synchronicity
defect obeys $\delta_{\mathrm{sync}} \leq 4\eps$ rather than $3\eps$, and one corner block
pairs with a transpose --- none checked here, none affecting the statement above, all
relevant to whoever writes its proof.


\subsubsection{The shape of the soundness proof}

\begin{remark}[How the soundness proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:qld} is organized]
\label{rem:qld-architecture}
\uses{thm:qld,thm:lidt-cl-soundness,rem:lidt-cl-adapter,thm:orthonormalization,lem:ms-direct-anticomm}
The soundness half of Theorem~\ref{thm:qld} is developed in the five-file appendix of
the pinned paper~\cite{AuditSync26}. Its
architecture is worth stating before its pieces, because the pieces are unreadable without it.
There are five stages.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Preliminaries}: three utility lemmas about closeness, one of which
  (Corollary~\ref{cor:ortho-from-consistency}) is used on almost every page.
\item \textbf{Win implications}: unpack ``succeeds with probability $1 - \eps$'' into one
  consistency or commutation statement per subtest (Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}).
\item \textbf{Expansion}: adjoin EPR ancillas and replace the strategy's measurements by ones
  on the enlarged space that approximately commute across the two bases
  (Lemmas~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-points} and~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-lines}).
\item \textbf{Combining}: build a \emph{single} measurement returning both the $X$- and the
  $Z$-polynomial, by padding the ambient space and applying seeded CL soundness to
  the padded strategy (Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-simultaneous}).
\item \textbf{Separation and the swap isometry}: extract exact Pauli operators from that
  single measurement and rotate them onto honest EPR pairs
  (Lemmas~\ref{lem:qld-exact-paulis} and~\ref{lem:qld-swap}).
\end{enumerate}
Stage 4 consumes Theorem~\ref{thm:lidt-cl-soundness} at $(q,4m,d,1)$, after
constructing a strategy for the seeded CL game and projectivizing its line POVMs.
Theorem~\ref{thm:lidt-soundness} justifies this application through
Theorem~\ref{thm:lidt-cl-soundness-one}, the $\mathrm{ldc}=1$ case being exactly what stage 4
consumes; what stage 4 must still supply is the padding itself. Stage 5 recovers the Pauli group
relations exactly, which permits the standard swap isometry; the expanded
measurements in stage 3 satisfy only approximate commutation.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Preliminaries}

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[Anticommuting question tuples]
\label{fact:omega-anticomm-prob}
\uses{def:ld-encoding,lem:schwartz-zippel}
\lean{MIPRE.LowDegree.indVec, MIPRE.LowDegree.indPair, MIPRE.LowDegree.indPair_eq_eval,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.acGamma, MIPRE.LowDegree.acTuples, MIPRE.LowDegree.cTuples,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.card_acTuples_add_card_cTuples,
  MIPRE.LowDegree.prob_anticommuting_ge, MIPRE.LowDegree.prob_commuting_ge}
\leanok
Let $\ind_m(x) \in \F_q^M$, $M = 2^m$, be the vector $(\ind_{m,y}(x))_y$ of the indicator
polynomials of Definition~\ref{def:ld-encoding} evaluated at a point $x \in \F_q^m$, and call
a tuple $\omega = (u_X, u_Z, r_X, r_Z) \in (\F_q^m)^2 \times \F_q^2$ \emph{anticommuting} if
\begin{equation*}
  \gamma(\omega) \;=\; \fieldtr_{q \to 2}\bigl( (r_X \cdot \ind_m(u_X)) \cdot
    (r_Z \cdot \ind_m(u_Z)) \bigr) \;\neq\; 0\;,
\end{equation*}
the inner product being the one of $\F_q^M$, and \emph{commuting} otherwise. The two cases
partition the sample space. If $m \geq 1$ and $d \geq 1$ then a uniformly random $\omega$ is
anticommuting with probability at least $(1 - 3md/q)/2$, and commuting with probability at
least $(1 - 3md/q)/2$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:schwartz-zippel}
Because $\ind_m(x) \cdot \ind_m(z) = g_{\ind_m(x)}(z)$ --- one low-degree encoding evaluated at
the other point --- $\gamma(\omega) = \fieldtr_{q \to 2}(r_X r_Z \cdot g_{\ind_m(u_X)}(u_Z))$.
The vector $\ind_m(x)$ has coordinate sum $\prod_i (x_i + (1 - x_i)) = 1$, so it is never zero
and $g_{\ind_m(u_X)}$ is a nonzero multilinear polynomial; by
Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} it vanishes at no more than an $m/q$ fraction of the $u_Z$.
Fix $u_X, u_Z$ with $g_{\ind_m(u_X)}(u_Z) \neq 0$ and $r_X \neq 0$, and put
$c = r_X\, g_{\ind_m(u_X)}(u_Z) \neq 0$. Then $r \mapsto \fieldtr_{q \to 2}(c r)$ is a nonzero
$\F_2$-linear functional on $\F_q$, so its kernel is a hyperplane and exactly $q/2$ of the
$r_Z$ give $\gamma \neq 0$, the other $q/2$ giving $\gamma = 0$. Multiplying the three
independent estimates gives $(1 - 1/q)(1 - m/q)/2 \geq (1 - (m+1)/q)/2$ for the anticommuting
case; the commuting case gains the whole $r_X = 0$ slice and the whole $g_{\ind_m(u_X)}(u_Z) =
0$ slice, so $(1 - 1/q)/2$ holds for it with no $m$ at all. Finally $m + 1 \leq 3md$ and
$1 \leq 3md$ when $m, d \geq 1$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Two departures from the paper's statement and proof, both recorded in
the formalization's own docstring. First, the paper states the bound as
$(1 - q^{-m})(1 - q^{-1})(1 - md/q)/2$; its own correction note removes the case distinction on
$u_X = 0$ that produced the first factor, observing that $\ind_m(u_X)$ is never the zero
vector, and keeps the factor only because it remains a valid lower bound. It is dropped here.
Second, the low-degree encoding is \emph{multilinear}, so Schwartz--Zippel costs $m/q$ and not
$md/q$; the formalized bounds are $(1 - (m+1)/q)/2$ and $(1 - 1/q)/2$, and the paper's
$(1 - 3md/q)/2$ is derived from them for $m, d \geq 1$. The `$d$' in this fact is therefore
inert: it is the individual-degree parameter of the surrounding test, and the bound does not
depend on it.

The complementarity statement is not decoration. Each of the two bounds is a lower bound on a
probability, so either alone would be implied by the wrong definition of its event; that they
hold for complementary events, and add to one, is what pins them down.

\effortEasy

\begin{lemma}[Averaging preserves closeness]
\label{lem:qld-averaging}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.1}
\uses{def:state-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.stateSqNorm_avg_le}
\leanok
Let $A^x \approx_\delta B^x$ on average over $x \sim \mu$, and let $\alpha_x$ be coefficients
with $\abs{\alpha_x} \leq 1$. Then $\E_x \alpha_x A^x \approx_\delta \E_x \alpha_x B^x$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Triangle inequality and Jensen. Note the hypothesis is $\abs{\alpha_x} \leq 1$, not
$\abs{\alpha_x} = 1$: the lemma is used with genuinely contractive coefficients. The
$\delta$ is preserved exactly, with no constant: the triangle inequality gives
$\norm{\E_x \alpha_x (A^x - B^x)\ket{\psi}} \leq \E_x \norm{(A^x - B^x)\ket{\psi}}$ and Jensen
for the square root gives $\E_x \sqrt{f(x)} \leq \sqrt{\E_x f(x)}$, which is the
$\mu$-probability case of \texttt{MIPRE.sum\_mul\_sqrt\_le}.
\end{proof}

\effortEasy

\begin{lemma}[From POVM elements to generalized observables]
\label{lem:qld-povm-to-obs}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.2}
\uses{def:state-distance,lem:qld-averaging}
\lean{MIPRE.obsOf, MIPRE.stateDist_obsOf_le}
\leanok
Let $\{A^x_a\}$ and $\{B^x_a\}$ be POVMs with a common outcome set $\mA$, and let $\alpha_a$
be coefficients of unit modulus. If $A^x_a \approx_\delta B^x_a$ then the generalized
observables $A^x = \sum_a \alpha_a A^x_a$ and $B^x = \sum_a \alpha_a B^x_a$ satisfy
$A^x \approx_{\abs{\mA} \delta} B^x$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
Triangle inequality over the outcomes, then Cauchy--Schwarz against the constant $1$:
$(\sum_a t_a)^2 \leq \abs{\mA} \sum_a t_a^2$. Unit modulus is more than the proof uses; the
formalized hypothesis is $\abs{\alpha_a} \leq 1$, and the constant $\abs{\mA}$ is exact rather
than an $O(\cdot)$.
\end{proof}

\effortMedium

\begin{corollary}[Orthonormalization from consistency]
\label{cor:ortho-from-consistency}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.3, 1.2.2.3.1}
\uses{thm:orthonormalization,def:inconsistency,def:state-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.exists_projective_of_consistent}
\leanok
Let $\delta > 0$, let $\{Q_a\}$ be a POVM on $\mH_\alice$ and $\{R_a\}$ a POVM on $\mH_\bob$
whose inconsistency on the unit vector $\ket{\psi}$ is at most $\delta$. Then there is a
\emph{projective} measurement $\{P_a\}$ on $\mH_\alice$ with
\begin{equation*}
  \sum_a \norm{\bigl((Q_a - P_a) \ot \Id_\bob\bigr)\ket{\psi}}^2 \;<\; 18\,\delta\;,
\end{equation*}
that is, $P_a \ot \Id \approx_{\eta_{ortho}(\delta)} Q_a \ot \Id$ on $\ket{\psi}$ with
$\eta_{ortho}(\delta) = 18\delta$, linear in $\delta$ as
Theorem~\ref{thm:orthonormalization} permits.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{thm:orthonormalization}
This is not Theorem~\ref{thm:orthonormalization} restated: that theorem requires strict
near-projectivity in a normal state, while what the appendix has is
bipartite consistency. The bridge is a Cauchy--Schwarz step. Write
$\gamma = \sum_{a \neq b} \bra{\psi} Q_a \ot R_b \ket{\psi}$, so that
$\sum_a \bra{\psi} Q_a \ot R_a \ket{\psi} = 1 - \gamma$ because
$\sum_{a,b} Q_a \ot R_b = \Id$. Since $\sum_a R_a^2 \leq \sum_a R_a = \Id$, Cauchy--Schwarz
over $a$ gives
\begin{equation*}
  1 - \gamma \;=\; \sum_a \bigl\langle (Q_a \ot \Id)\ket{\psi},\,
    (\Id \ot R_a)\ket{\psi} \bigr\rangle
  \;\leq\; \Bigl(\sum_a \bra{\psi} Q_a^2 \ot \Id \ket{\psi}\Bigr)^{1/2}\;,
\end{equation*}
hence $\sum_a \bra{\psi} Q_a^2 \ot \Id \ket{\psi} \geq (1 - \gamma)^2 = 1 - 2\gamma + \gamma^2$.
Now $2\gamma - \gamma^2 < 2\delta$ for every $\gamma \leq \delta$ with $\delta > 0$ --- at
$\gamma = \delta$ because $\gamma^2 > 0$, and at $\gamma < \delta$ because $\gamma^2 \geq 0$ ---
and if $\gamma \geq 1$ the hypothesis holds vacuously, so near-projectivity at $\eps = 2\delta$
holds \emph{strictly}. Apply Theorem~\ref{thm:orthonormalization} to
$\cal{L}(\mH_\alice)\ot\Id$ with the vector state of $\ket{\psi}$: it gives a projective family
$P_a \ot \Id$ with squared-distance sum less than $9\eps = 18\delta$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The paper closes this proof differently, and the difference is a
\textbf{repair}. It derives near-projectivity at $2\gamma$ for the \emph{actual} inconsistency
$\gamma$, which is non-strict, so Theorem~\ref{thm:orthonormalization} applies only at each
$\eps > 2\gamma$; it then takes $\eps \downarrow 2\gamma$ inside the (compact) set of projective
measurements with a fixed finite outcome set and extracts a convergent subsequence to reach
$18\gamma$ itself. That limit is not needed. The slack $\gamma^2$ in
$(1-\gamma)^2 = 1 - 2\gamma + \gamma^2$ already makes the hypothesis strict at $\eps = 2\delta$
for any \emph{bound} $\delta > 0$ on the inconsistency, which is the form in which every
application supplies it and the form stated above; the compactness argument, and with it the
continuity of $\{P_a\} \mapsto \sum_a \norm{((Q_a - P_a)\ot\Id)\ket{\psi}}^2$ and the
sequential extraction, disappears. What the paper's version additionally gives is the exact
case $\delta = 0$, where the limit produces a projective measurement agreeing with $\{Q_a\}$ on
$\ket{\psi}$ exactly. Nothing downstream uses it: every invocation carries a positive error
bound. The statement above is therefore weaker only at $\delta = 0$ and stronger elsewhere,
both in being strict and in naming the constant.

\subsubsection{What winning implies}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Win implications]
\label{lem:qld-win}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.4, 1.2.2.4.2}
\uses{def:qld-game,def:cross-distance,fact:omega-anticomm-prob,lem:ms-direct-anticomm,
  def:magic-square,def:ld-encoding,def:lidt-cl}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.subtest_le, MIPRE.QLD.agree_subtest_le, MIPRE.QLD.item_consistency,
  MIPRE.QLD.item_lowDeg_aline, MIPRE.QLD.item_lowDeg_dline,
  MIPRE.QLD.item_pauli_consistency, MIPRE.QLD.item_commutation,
  MIPRE.QLD.item_commutation_consistency, MIPRE.QLD.item_ms_consistency_X,
  MIPRE.QLD.item_ms_consistency_Z, MIPRE.QLD.msPOVM, MIPRE.QLD.msEps,
  MIPRE.QLD.condFail_ms_le, MIPRE.QLD.one_sub_povmValue_ms_le, MIPRE.QLD.sum_msEps_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.item_magicSquare}
\leanok
Let $\strategy = (\ket{\psi}, M)$ be a POVM strategy succeeding with probability at least
$1 - \eps$ in the Pauli basis test $\game^\pauli_\qldparams$ of
Definition~\ref{def:qld-game}. Let $\gamma(\omega)$ be as in
Lemma~\ref{fact:omega-anticomm-prob}, write $g_h$ for the low-degree encoding of
Definition~\ref{def:ld-encoding}, and let every average below be over the verifier's content,
each content carrying the same weight, restricted where indicated to the contents whose tuple
$\omega$ is commuting ($\gamma(\omega) = 0$) or anticommuting ($\gamma(\omega) \neq 0$). Then,
with $\simeq_\delta$ the cross-party distance of Definition~\ref{def:cross-distance}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item (\textbf{Consistency}) for every question type $t$,
  $M^{t,x}_a \ot \Id \simeq_{172\eps} \Id \ot M^{t,x}_a$;
\item (\textbf{Low degree}) for each $W \in \{X, Z\}$ and each of the two line types, the line
  answer evaluated at the sampled point agrees with the point answer:
  $M^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u_W}_a \ot \Id \simeq_{172\eps}
    \Id \ot M^{(\mathtt{Line}, W), \ell}_{[\,f(t) = a\,]}$, where $f$ is the polynomial
  answered and $t$ the parameter of $u_W$ on the line $\ell$;
\item (\textbf{Pauli basis consistency}) for each $W$,
  $M^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u_W}_a \ot \Id \simeq_{172\eps}
    \Id \ot M^{(\pauli, W)}_{[g_h(u_W) = a]}$;
\item (\textbf{Commutation}) for each $W$, on average over \emph{commuting} $\omega$,
  $M^{(\mathtt{Pair}, W), \omega}_b \ot \Id \simeq_{172\eps}
    \Id \ot M^{\mathtt{Pair}, \omega}_{[\beta_W = b]}$;
\item (\textbf{Commutation consistency}) for each $W$, on average over commuting $\omega$,
  $M^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u_W}_{[\fieldtr(\cdot\, r_W) = b]} \ot \Id \simeq_{172\eps}
    \Id \ot M^{(\mathtt{Pair}, W), \omega}_b$;
\item (\textbf{Magic square}) on average over \emph{anticommuting} $\omega$, the
  $\mathtt{Constraint}$ and $\mathtt{Variable}$ measurements at $\omega$ form a strategy for the
  Magic Square game of value $1 - O(\eps)$; consequently, by
  Lemma~\ref{lem:ms-direct-anticomm},
  \begin{equation*}
    \E_{\omega \,:\, \gamma(\omega) \neq 0}
      \norm{\bigl(\Id \ot \{B^{\mathtt{Variable}_1, \omega},
        B^{\mathtt{Variable}_5, \omega}\}\bigr) \ket{\psi}}^2 \;\leq\; 16049664\,\eps\;;
  \end{equation*}
\item (\textbf{Magic square consistency}) on average over anticommuting $\omega$,
  $M^{(\mathtt{Point}, X), u_X}_{[\fieldtr(\cdot\, r_X) = b]} \ot \Id \simeq_{172\eps}
    \Id \ot M^{\mathtt{Variable}_1, \omega}_b$, and the same with $Z$, $r_Z$ and
  $\mathtt{Variable}_5$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:ms-direct-anticomm,def:cross-distance}
Items 1--5 and 7 are one lemma applied seven times. The question distribution factors as an
ordered type edge times the content, both uniform, so a question pair arising from a fixed edge
of the type graph carries at least $1/86$ of the mass --- $86$ being the ordered pairs counted in
Definition~\ref{def:qld-game} --- and the conditional failure of any subtest is therefore at
most $86\eps$. Each rule of $\decider^\pauli$ accepts only when two named readings of the two
answers agree, so~\eqref{eq:cross-from-agreement} converts that into $172\eps$ of cross-party
distance for the measurements coarse-grained along those readings. The four rules gated on
$\gamma$ check nothing on the other value of $\gamma$, which is why their averages are
restricted.

Item 6 is not of that shape. Relabelling the $\mathtt{Constraint}_i$ and $\mathtt{Variable}_j$
answers at a fixed $\omega$ into the Magic Square game's alphabet --- the three bits placed at
the constraint's cells, the bit passed through --- gives a strategy for $\game^\MS$, and
relabelling outcomes is data processing, so that strategy wins whenever the Pauli test's own rule
would have accepted. Its failure is therefore at most the Magic Square game's own
question-weighted sum of the Pauli test's conditional failures at the corresponding question
pairs, which is $86\eps$ on average over anticommuting $\omega$ --- one factor of $86$ and not
thirty-six, because $\game^\MS$'s distribution is itself normalized. Lemma~\ref{lem:ms-direct-anticomm}
applied to each such conditional strategy and averaged gives $186624 \cdot 86 = 16049664$. This
uses the strategy's own observables and invokes no Magic Square rigidity.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Three departures from the paper's statement
(\cite{AuditSync26}, \texttt{qld-commutation.tex}, \texttt{lem:qld-win-implications}), and one
from this blueprint's own earlier text.

The paper's hypothesis $6md \leq q$ is \textbf{not needed} and is dropped. It is there to turn
the gated items into averages \emph{conditioned} on $\gamma$: by
Lemma~\ref{fact:omega-anticomm-prob} each of the two events has probability at least
$(1 - 3md/q)/2 \geq 1/4$ under it, so dividing by that probability costs a factor $4$. The
formalization states the gated items in the \emph{unconditional} form, with the gate's indicator
inside the average, which needs no lower bound on the gate's probability and is what the
machinery produces directly. Whoever needs the conditional form divides, and pays $6md \leq q$
there.

The constants are named rather than hidden in $O(\eps)$. They are $172 = 2 \cdot 86$ for the
consistency items --- the factor $2$ of~\eqref{eq:cross-from-agreement} and the selection
probability $1/86$ --- and $16049664 = 186624 \cdot 86$ for the anticommutator. Item 6 in the
paper reads $\E \Lambda_\omega = 1 - O(\eps)$; that intermediate form is
\texttt{one\_sub\_povmValue\_ms\_le} together with \texttt{sum\_msEps\_le}, and the item above
states the composition with Lemma~\ref{lem:ms-direct-anticomm} because that is what the
expansion stage consumes.

The paper's item 2 quantifies over the line--point distribution of a separate definition; here it
is the two line types of Definition~\ref{def:qld-game} separately, at the questions the content
actually produces, which is the form Definition~\ref{def:lidt-cl}'s own decider is stated in and
avoids a second distribution.

Finally, this blueprint's earlier statement of this lemma bundled into it the transfer of the
anticommutation to the \emph{point} observables. That is the paper's separate
\texttt{lem:qld-win-implications-obs}, it needs items 4, 5, 6 and 7 together, and it is where
the sign $(-1)^{\gamma}$ first appears; it belongs with the expansion stage that consumes it and
is not part of this lemma.

\subsubsection{The point observables}

The stage that follows works with the $\pm 1$-observables of the two-outcome probes rather than
with the measurements themselves, and needs two statements about them: they are cross-party
consistent, and they commute up to the sign $(-1)^{\gamma}$. The first is
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win} item 1 and costs one factor of two; the second is the synthesis of items
4 to 7 and is where the sign that Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-points} cancels first appears.

\effortEasy

\begin{lemma}[Consistency of the point observables]
\label{lem:qld-obs-consistency}
\uses{lem:qld-win,def:cross-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.pts_obs_consistency}
\leanok
For $W \in \{X, Z\}$ and $r \in \F_q$ write
$W^r(u) = M^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u}_{[\fieldtr(\cdot\, r) = 0]}
  - M^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u}_{[\fieldtr(\cdot\, r) = 1]}$
for the $\pm 1$-observable of the probe. Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}, on
average over the verifier's content,
\begin{equation*}
  W^r(u) \ot \Id_\bob \;\approx_{344\eps}\; \Id_\alice \ot W^r(u)\;.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-win,def:cross-distance}
Item 1 of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win} holds for any \emph{common} reading of the two answers, the
rule at equal types accepting only when the answers are equal and equal answers having equal
readings --- so the data-processing step the paper cites separately is already in the item. Take
the reading to be the probe $a \mapsto \fieldtr(a\,r)$ and apply
\texttt{xStateDist\_obsOf\_le}, the cross-party form of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-povm-to-obs}, with
the two signs $\pm 1$: the factor is the number of outcomes of the probe, which is two, giving
$2 \cdot 172\eps$.
\end{proof}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Signed commutation of the point observables]
\label{lem:qld-obs-commutation}
\uses{lem:qld-win,lem:qld-obs-consistency,fact:omega-anticomm-prob,cor:ortho-from-consistency,
  lem:commutation-analysis,def:cross-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.signed_commutation}
\leanok
Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}, on average over uniformly random
$\omega = (u_X, u_Z, r_X, r_Z)$,
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:qld-obs-commutation-bp}
  X^{r_X}(u_X)\, Z^{r_Z}(u_Z) \ot \Id_\bob
    \;\approx_{57676416\,\eps}\;
  (-1)^{\gamma(\omega)}\; Z^{r_Z}(u_Z)\, X^{r_X}(u_X) \ot \Id_\bob\;.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-win,lem:qld-obs-consistency,lem:commutation-analysis,def:cross-distance,
  cor:ortho-from-consistency}
Two cases, and the sign is the branch. The content distribution is uniform and the two cases
partition it, so the statement is the sum of the two restricted ones:
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-obs-commutation-comm} at $9519168\,\eps$ and
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-obs-commutation-acomm} at $48157248\,\eps$.
\end{proof}

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[Signed commutation, the commuting case]
\label{lem:qld-obs-commutation-comm}
\uses{lem:qld-win,cor:ortho-from-consistency,lem:commutation-analysis,def:cross-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.pairPOVM, MIPRE.QLD.pairPOVMB, MIPRE.QLD.ptPOVM, MIPRE.QLD.ptPOVMB,
  MIPRE.QLD.ptObsB, MIPRE.QLD.ptObs_eq_obs2, MIPRE.QLD.ptObsB_mul_self_le_one,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_pairPOVM_X, MIPRE.QLD.sum_pairPOVM_Z, MIPRE.QLD.adj_pairB_point,
  MIPRE.QLD.link_pairB_point, MIPRE.QLD.link_pair_pair, MIPRE.QLD.chain_pair_point,
  MIPRE.QLD.chainQty, MIPRE.QLD.incQty, MIPRE.QLD.chainQty_nonneg, MIPRE.QLD.incQty_nonneg,
  MIPRE.QLD.commWeight, MIPRE.QLD.commWeight_nonneg, MIPRE.QLD.sum_commWeight_mul,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_commWeight_le_one, MIPRE.QLD.xStateDist_ptObs_ptObsB,
  MIPRE.QLD.pairInconsistency_nonneg, MIPRE.QLD.incQty_le_condFail, MIPRE.QLD.sum_incQty_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.sq_norm_ptObs_comm_le_of_content, MIPRE.QLD.sum_chainQty_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.comm_signed_commutation}
\leanok
Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}, summed over the \emph{commuting} tuples with the
uniform weight $\abs{\mC}^{-1}$,
\begin{equation*}
  \sum_{\omega \,:\, \gamma(\omega) = 0} \frac{1}{\abs{\mC}}
    \norm{\bigl( [X^{r_X}(u_X),\, Z^{r_Z}(u_Z)] \bigr) \ot \Id_\bob \ket{\psi}}^2
  \;\leq\; 9519168\, \eps\;.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-win,cor:ortho-from-consistency,lem:commutation-analysis,def:cross-distance}
Items 5, 4 and 1 chain each point probe to a \emph{marginal} of the $\mathtt{Pair}$ measurement on
the other side: three links at $172\eps$ each, so $1548\eps$ after a three-term triangle
inequality. Corollary~\ref{cor:ortho-from-consistency} replaces that measurement by a projective
one at $18$ times the inconsistency of the two players' $\mathtt{Pair}$ measurements, which item 1
bounds at the Born level by $86\eps$; marginalizing costs a factor two per marginal. Then
Lemma~\ref{lem:commutation-analysis} applies, and \eqref{eq:anticomm-transfer-bp}'s commutator form
with Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-obs-consistency}'s probe carries the conclusion to the observables. The
constant is $12 \cdot 344 + 12 \cdot 344 + 768 \cdot (2 \cdot 1548 + 2 \cdot 1548 + 72 \cdot 86)$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Commutation.lean}. Two departures
from the paper's bookkeeping, both forced by which side an object lives on.

\textbf{The analysis is run on Bob's observables and carried back.}
Lemma~\ref{lem:commutation-analysis} wants the two commuting families on one side and the joint
projective measurement on the \emph{other}, and Corollary~\ref{cor:ortho-from-consistency} produces
a projective measurement on \emph{Alice's} factor. So the analysis is applied to Bob's point
measurements against Alice's projectivized $\mathtt{Pair}$ measurement, and the conclusion is
carried to Alice's observables by the order-reversal rule. The alternative --- a Bob-side
orthonormalization corollary --- would duplicate \texttt{Ortho.lean} through the swap.

\textbf{The chain is run in the mirrored orientation.} Every rule of \texttt{fig:decider\_pauli} is
stated in both orientations, so items 5 and 1 are available with the players exchanged and item 4 is
used as it stands.

\textbf{Data processing is a consistency-level fact.} The chain's links are converted to the
state-dependent distance only after the coarse-grainings are taken, because $\approx_\delta$
\emph{has no data-processing inequality}: NW19's Fact 4.26 is stated for $\simeq_\delta$, and the
remark following it gives a counterexample for $\approx_\delta$. The paper's
\texttt{fact:data-processing} is the $\simeq$ form, correctly; a formalization that reached for the
$\approx$ form would be proving something false.

\textbf{The slack in Corollary~\ref{cor:ortho-from-consistency} is discharged by a limit.} That
corollary needs a \emph{strictly positive} bound $\delta$ on the inconsistency. Here it is applied
at $\delta = \text{(the inconsistency)} + \eta$ for arbitrary $\eta > 0$; $\eta$ survives the
averaging as an additive constant and is sent to zero at the end
(\texttt{le\_of\_forall\_pos\_le\_add}). No compactness is needed, which is the same observation the
corollary's own file records.

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[Signed commutation, the anticommuting case]
\label{lem:qld-obs-commutation-acomm}
\uses{lem:qld-win,lem:qld-obs-consistency,fact:omega-anticomm-prob,def:cross-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.ptObs, MIPRE.QLD.varObs, MIPRE.QLD.ptObs_mul_self_le_one,
  MIPRE.QLD.varObs_mul_self_le_one, MIPRE.QLD.acommWeight, MIPRE.QLD.acommWeight_nonneg,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_acommWeight_mul, MIPRE.QLD.xStateDist_ptObs_varObs_X,
  MIPRE.QLD.xStateDist_ptObs_varObs_Z, MIPRE.QLD.question_var, MIPRE.QLD.mats_msPOVM_var,
  MIPRE.QLD.bobs_msPOVM, MIPRE.QLD.anti_eq_varObs, MIPRE.QLD.sq_norm_ptObs_anticomm_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.acomm_signed_commutation}
\leanok
Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}, summed over the \emph{anticommuting} tuples with
the uniform weight $\abs{\mC}^{-1}$ on the verifier's content,
\begin{equation*}
  \sum_{\omega \,:\, \gamma(\omega) = 1} \frac{1}{\abs{\mC}}
    \norm{\bigl( X^{r_X}(u_X)\, Z^{r_Z}(u_Z) + Z^{r_Z}(u_Z)\, X^{r_X}(u_X) \bigr) \ot \Id_\bob
      \ket{\psi}}^2
  \;\leq\; 16049664\, \eps + 8256\, \eps \;=\; 48157248\, \eps\;,
\end{equation*}
which is Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-obs-commutation} at $(-1)^{\gamma(\omega)} = -1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-win,def:cross-distance}
Item 7 of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win} says that on an anticommuting tuple each point probe agrees
with one of the two distinguished $\mathtt{Variable}$ bits on the other side. At the level of
observables that is $X^{r_X}(u_X) \ot \Id \simeq_{344\eps} \Id \ot V_1$ and
$Z^{r_Z}(u_Z) \ot \Id \simeq_{344\eps} \Id \ot V_5$ --- item 7 at the two-outcome probe, then
\texttt{xStateDist\_obsOf\_le}, whose cost is the number of outcomes of the probe, two. Item 6
says $V_1$ and $V_5$ anticommute on the state at $16049664\,\eps$. Feed the three
into~\eqref{eq:anticomm-transfer-bp} and square with
$(a+b+c)^2 \leq 3(a^2+b^2+c^2)$: the constant is
$12 \cdot 344 + 12 \cdot 344 + 3 \cdot 16049664$.

Two bookkeeping points. The weight is uniform on all contents but supported on the anticommuting
ones, which is how every item of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win} is stated, so it is a sub-probability and
not a distribution; \texttt{sum\_acommWeight\_mul} is the one lemma that converts between the two
forms. And item 6 is stated for the anticommutator of the \emph{conditional Magic Square
strategy}'s observables, which are the same operators as the $\mathtt{Variable}$ observables here:
the conditional strategy at a $\mathtt{Variable}$ question is the Pauli test's measurement at the
corresponding question with its answer relabelled to the bit it reports, and relabelling along the
injection $\mathtt{Sum.inr}$ leaves a coarse-grained POVM element alone
(\texttt{bobs\_msPOVM}).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Commutation.lean}.

\textbf{This half costs $\eps$, not $\sqrt{\eps}$.} Every input above is a squared-norm bound
linear in $\eps$, and the only inequalities used are the triangle inequality and
$(a+b+c)^2 \leq 3(a^2+b^2+c^2)$, so nothing introduces a square root. \emph{Correction to an
earlier reading of this:} the square root in~\eqref{eq:qld-obs-commutation-bp} as the paper writes
it does \emph{not} belong to the commuting case either --- that case is $O(\eps)$ too
(Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-obs-commutation-comm}), since \eqref{eq:commutation-analysis-bp} is linear in
its hypothesis and Corollary~\ref{cor:ortho-from-consistency} is linear in the inconsistency. The
paper's $\sqrt{\eps}$ is a uniform \emph{weakening}, and its own text says so where the
anticommuting case is proved: ``direct averaging proves the anticommutation relation with squared
error $O(\eps)$. In particular it implies the following weaker bound, retained in the form used
below''. Nothing downstream needs the sharper form --- \texttt{lem:qld-comm-cons} asks only for
$\poly(\eps)$ --- so this is a note about where a square root is \emph{not} spent, not a repair.

\textbf{The sign is the double reversal.} Both order reversals run in the same direction --- a
product transferred across the tensor factors comes back reversed --- and what sits between them
is Bob's \emph{anti}commutator, a sum. So the two reversals do not cancel: Alice's two observables
anticommute because Bob's do, and that is the $(-1)^{\gamma}$ the expansion stage of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-points} cancels against the ancillas.

\subsubsection{Expansion: approximate commutation of the two bases}

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Expanded point measurements]
\label{lem:qld-expanded-points}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.5}
\uses{lem:qld-win,cor:ortho-from-consistency,def:distance,def:expanded-state,
  lem:qld-expanded-commutation,lem:qld-expanded-consistency}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.expanded_points}
\leanok
Adjoin EPR ancilla registers to $\ket{\psi}$. There are measurements
$\widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u}$ on each player's space together with its ancilla such
that, with $\delta_{\mathrm{acom}}(\eps) = O(\sqrt{\eps})$: they are self-consistent across
the two sides at error $\eps$, and the $X$-side and $Z$-side coarse observables
\emph{approximately commute} on the expanded state at error $\delta_{\mathrm{acom}}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-win,lem:qld-expanded-commutation,lem:qld-expanded-consistency}
Two items, one lemma each. The pivotal one is an exact cancellation of a sign $(-1)^\gamma$ between
the strategy's own observables, supplied by the anticommutation relations of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}, and the ancilla observables: Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-commutation}.
Without the ancillas the two bases only commute up to an error carrying that sign, which does not
improve with $\eps$; with them the sign cancels identically. Self-consistency is
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-consistency}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Both items are formalized, at $172\eps$ and $57676416\eps$; the
$\delta_{\mathrm{acom}} = O(\sqrt{\eps})$ of the statement is a weakening, as it is one step
earlier (see Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-obs-commutation-acomm}'s comments). The expansion contributes
\emph{nothing} to either constant, which is the point of
Definition~\ref{def:expanded-state}'s items 3 and 4.

\begin{lemma}[Expanded point measurements are self-consistent]
\label{lem:qld-expanded-consistency}
\uses{lem:qld-win,def:generalized-pauli,def:weyl-epr,def:expanded-state}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.synPOVM, MIPRE.QLD.synPOVM_mats, MIPRE.QLD.weylOf_transpose,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_bornProb_epr_synPOVM, MIPRE.QLD.ptValPOVM, MIPRE.QLD.hatPOVM,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_xSqNorm_hatPOVM_le, MIPRE.QLD.hatPOVM_consistency}
\leanok
With $\ket{\hat\psi}$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-commutation}, let
\begin{equation*}
  \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point},W),u}_a
    \;=\; \sum_{a' + a'' = a} M^{(\mathtt{Point},W),u}_{a'} \ot \qp^{W}_{[\,\cdot\, \ind_m(u) = a'']}
\end{equation*}
be the convolution of the strategy's point measurement with the ancilla's syndrome measurement.
Then, on average over the verifier's content,
\begin{equation*}
  \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point},W),u}_a \ot \Id \;\simeq_{172\eps}\;
    \Id \ot \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point},W),u}_a\;.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-win,def:weyl-epr,def:expanded-state}
Three inputs and no loss. The agreement probability of a product measurement on the expanded state
factorizes (Definition~\ref{def:expanded-state}); the ancilla factor is \emph{exactly one}, because
the syndrome projectors are perfectly self-consistent across the two halves of
$\ket{\EPR_q}^{\ot M}$ --- that is Definition~\ref{def:weyl-epr}'s stabilizer relation, summed over
the level set, and it is $1$ rather than $1 - O(\eps)$; and the convolution is a coarse-graining, so
it can only \emph{increase} agreement. What is left is the disagreement of the strategy's own point
measurements, which item 1 of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win} bounds by the conditional failure of the
$(\mathtt{Point}, W)$ subtest. One conversion from agreement to distance
(\eqref{eq:cross-from-agreement}) gives $2 \cdot 86 \eps$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Expanded.lean}. The whole estimate
runs on Born probabilities and is converted to the state-dependent distance exactly once, at the
end. That is not a stylistic choice: the convolution is a coarse-graining, and
$\approx_\delta$ has no data-processing inequality (Definition~\ref{def:expanded-state}'s comments).
So the route through agreement probabilities is the only correct one, and it is also the one that
makes the ancilla's contribution visible as an exact $1$.

\begin{lemma}[Expanded point observables commute]
\label{lem:qld-expanded-commutation}
\uses{lem:qld-obs-commutation,def:generalized-pauli,def:weyl-epr,def:expanded-state}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.Anc, MIPRE.QLD.weylOf, MIPRE.QLD.ancVec, MIPRE.QLD.gam_eq_trDot,
  MIPRE.QLD.weylOf_isUnitary, MIPRE.QLD.weylOf_prod_isUnitary, MIPRE.QLD.hatObs,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatVec, MIPRE.QLD.norm_evec_epr, MIPRE.QLD.hatVec_unit,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatObs_comm_eq, MIPRE.QLD.norm_hatVec_hatObs_comm,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatObs_commutation}
\leanok
Let $\ket{\hat\psi} = \ket{\psi} \ot \ket{\EPR_q}^{\ot M}$ with the ancilla halves split between
the two parties, and let
$\widehat{W}^{r}(u) = W^{r}(u) \ot \qp^W(r \cdot \ind_m(u))$. Then, on average over the verifier's
content and with \emph{no sign},
\begin{equation*}
  \sum_\omega \frac{1}{\abs{\mC}}
    \norm{\bigl[ \widehat{X}^{r_X}(u_X),\, \widehat{Z}^{r_Z}(u_Z) \bigr]
      \ot \Id \ket{\hat\psi}}^2 \;\leq\; 57676416\, \eps\;.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-obs-commutation,def:generalized-pauli,def:weyl-epr,def:expanded-state}
One identity and two bookkeeping facts. The identity is
\begin{equation*}
  \bigl[ \widehat{X}^{r_X}(u_X),\, \widehat{Z}^{r_Z}(u_Z) \bigr]
  \;=\; \Bigl( X^{r_X}(u_X) Z^{r_Z}(u_Z) - (-1)^{\gamma(\omega)} Z^{r_Z}(u_Z) X^{r_X}(u_X) \Bigr)
    \ot \Bigl( \qp^X(r_X \cdot \ind_m(u_X))\, \qp^Z(r_Z \cdot \ind_m(u_Z)) \Bigr)\;,
\end{equation*}
because the two ancilla operators commute up to \emph{exactly} the sign $(-1)^{\gamma(\omega)}$ ---
the twisted commutation relation of Definition~\ref{def:generalized-pauli}, whose sign is the form
$\fieldtr\bigl( (r_X \ind_m(u_X)) \cdot (r_Z \ind_m(u_Z)) \bigr)$, and that form \emph{is}
$\gamma(\omega)$ (\texttt{gam\_eq\_trDot}). Then the surviving ancilla operator is unitary, hence
invisible to the state-norm, and what is left has an inert ancilla, hence the same norm on
$\ket{\hat\psi}$ as on $\ket{\psi}$ (Definition~\ref{def:expanded-state}, items 3 and 4). So the
bound is exactly Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-obs-commutation}'s, with the sign removed.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Expanded.lean}. This is what the
expansion exists for, and it is worth saying precisely what is bought: \emph{the sign is gone, not
smaller}. Without the ancillas the two bases commute only up to an error carrying
$(-1)^{\gamma(\omega)}$, which no amount of soundness improves, because $\gamma$ is a property of
the question and not of the strategy. With them the two signs multiply to one identically, and the
error that remains is the strategy's own.

The constant carries over unchanged. That is the content of items 3 and 4 of
Definition~\ref{def:expanded-state}: the expansion is free, in the sense that no step of it loses
anything, which is also why it is stated there rather than here.

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Expanded line measurements]
\label{lem:qld-expanded-lines}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.6}
\uses{lem:qld-win,lem:qld-expanded-points,def:weyl-epr,def:expanded-state,def:line-restriction,
  def:lidt-cl}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.lineCoeffs, MIPRE.QLD.eval_lineCoeffs, MIPRE.QLD.dotF_indVec,

  MIPRE.QLD.rdLine, MIPRE.QLD.synLinePOVM, MIPRE.QLD.synLinePOVM_map_eval,
  MIPRE.QLD.lineAnsPOVM, MIPRE.QLD.hatLinePOVM, MIPRE.QLD.isPVM_hatLinePOVM,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatLinePOVM_map_eval, MIPRE.QLD.sum_xSqNorm_hatLinePOVM_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatLinePOVM_consistency, MIPRE.QLD.Content.omega_pt,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_xSqNorm_hatLine_point_le, MIPRE.QLD.rep_add_lineParam_smul,
  MIPRE.QLD.abaseOf, MIPRE.QLD.ddirOf, MIPRE.QLD.dbaseOf, MIPRE.QLD.d_le_mul,
  MIPRE.QLD.m_le_mul, MIPRE.QLD.hsub_aline, MIPRE.QLD.hsub_dline,

Let $d \geq 1$ and let $\ket{\hat\psi}$ be as in Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-commutation}. For
each $W \in \{X, Z\}$ and each line type --- axis-parallel or diagonal --- write $\ell$ for the
line the verifier's content presents on the $W$ side, and let
\begin{equation*}
  \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, W), \ell}_f
    \;=\; \sum_{f' + f'' = f} M^{(\mathtt{Line}, W), \ell}_{f'} \ot \qp^{W, \ell}_{f''}
\end{equation*}
be the convolution of the strategy's line measurement with the ancilla measurement that reports
the restriction to $\ell$ of the low-degree encoding of its outcome
(Definition~\ref{def:line-restriction}), the outcomes being the polynomials of degree at most
$md$ on $\ell$. Then, on average over the verifier's content --- which is the line--point
distribution, the line and the point being read off the same sample:
\begin{enumerate}
\item (\textbf{Self-consistency})
  $\widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, W), \ell}_f \ot \Id \;\simeq_{172\eps}\;
    \Id \ot \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, W), \ell}_f$;
\item (\textbf{Consistency with the expanded point measurements})
  $\widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u}_a \ot \Id \;\simeq_{172\eps}\;
    \Id \ot \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, W), \ell}_{[\mathrm{eval}_u(\cdot) = a]}$,
  where $u$ is the point of the same content and $\mathrm{eval}_u$ evaluates a polynomial on
  $\ell$ at the parameter of $u$.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover $\{\widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, W), \ell}_f\}_f$ is \emph{projective} whenever the
strategy's own line measurement is, which is without loss of generality by Naimark dilation.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-win,lem:qld-expanded-points,def:weyl-epr,def:expanded-state,def:line-restriction}
Everything is exact except the strategy's own error, and the two items come out of the same
three inputs as Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-consistency}.

Projectivity is two closure properties and no computation: a product of projective measurements is
projective, and so is any coarse-graining of one --- because the elements of a projective
measurement are mutually orthogonal, a sum of them over a level set is idempotent. The convolution
is a coarse-graining of a product, so it is projective.

For item 1, the agreement probability of the product measurement on the expanded state factorizes
(Definition~\ref{def:expanded-state}); the ancilla factor is \emph{exactly one}, the level-set
projectors of the basis measurement being perfectly self-consistent across the two halves of
$\ket{\EPR_q}^{\ot M}$ (Definition~\ref{def:weyl-epr}); the convolution can only increase
agreement; and what remains is item 1 of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win} at the line type's self-loop. One
conversion from agreement to distance gives $2 \cdot 86\eps$.

For item 2 the ancilla contributes an identity rather than an estimate: relabelling a line outcome
of the ancilla by its value at the point \emph{is} the ancilla's point measurement at that point.
Both are coarse-grainings of the same eigenbasis measurement, and the two labels agree because
evaluating the restriction of $g_h$ to $\ell$ at the parameter of $u$ is evaluating $g_h$ at $u$
(Definition~\ref{def:line-restriction}), $u$ lying on $\ell$ at that parameter. So the two
players' measurements are convolutions of their own factors with \emph{one} ancilla measurement,
the factorization applies again, the ancilla factor is again exactly one, and what remains is item
2a or 2b of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win} --- the line answer evaluated at the sampled point against the
point answer --- at $2 \cdot 86 \eps$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Expanded.lean}, with the
restriction operation in \texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/LowDegree/LineRestrict.lean} and the two
closure properties of projectivity in \texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/PVM.lean} and
\texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/Expanded.lean}.

Three things about the statement have changed against the version this replaces, and all three are
improvements rather than paraphrases.

$\delta_{\mathrm{Line}}$ is \emph{linear} in $\eps$, at the constant $172\eps$ of item 1 of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}, not $O(\sqrt\eps)$ as this lemma previously claimed. The paper says
$\poly(\eps)$ and is consistent with either; the square root was this blueprint's own and had no
source. Nothing in the proof takes a square root, because nothing in it converts an operator
inequality into a norm.

Projectivity does not come from Corollary~\ref{cor:ortho-from-consistency}, and that reference is
gone from the \texttt{uses} list. It comes from projectivity of the strategy's own line
measurement, which the paper takes for granted and which is without loss of generality; the
formalized statement carries it as a hypothesis rather than hiding it.

The paper's two forms of item 2 --- comparing a line outcome with a point outcome, and comparing
the \emph{evaluation} of the line polynomial at the point --- are one statement here. The paper
needs a direct calculation to pass between them, because it works with $\approx$ and
Fact~\texttt{fact:data-processing} is a statement about $\simeq$ (a defect it flags itself). At the
Born level the passage is free: summing the line outcomes over a fibre of $\mathrm{eval}_u$ is a
coarse-graining, which increases agreement, so the estimate proved is the stronger one and the
weaker follows without a separate argument.

One thing is deliberately \emph{not} claimed. For an axis-parallel line the honest restriction is
affine, hence of degree at most $d$ rather than $md$; that refinement is what
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-pairs-of-lines} will need in order to keep the padded answers in the
degree-$d$ format, and it needs the individual-degree bound along the line's own direction, not
the total-degree bound proved here. The convolution above is stated at degree $md$ for both line
types, with the axis-parallel answers padded, which is what the paper's printed definition does
too.

\subsubsection{Combining the two bases}

The point of this stage is a single measurement that returns the $X$-polynomial and the
$Z$-polynomial at once. It is reached by padding the ambient space from $\F_q^m$ to
$\F_q^{4m}$, so that a point of the padded space encodes a point in each basis together with
a pair of coefficients, and then applying the seeded CL theorem to the padded
strategy.

\effortHard

\begin{theorem}[Exact linearity from approximate linearity]
\label{thm:linearity}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.7, 1.2.2.7.1, 1.2.2.7.2, 1.2.2.7.3}
\uses{def:state-distance,def:generalized-pauli,lem:naimark-dilation}
\lean{MIPRE.fourierOf, MIPRE.fourierOf_conjTranspose, MIPRE.fourierOf_posSemidef,
  MIPRE.sum_sgn_smul_fourierOf_sq, MIPRE.sum_fourierOf_sq, MIPRE.exists_exactly_linear,
  MIPRE.extVecA, MIPRE.ancillaEmbed_kron_isometry, MIPRE.norm_evec_extVecA,
  MIPRE.kron_one_mul_ancillaEmbed, MIPRE.ancillaEmbed_conjTranspose_mul_kron_one,
  MIPRE.compress_mul_kron_one, MIPRE.compress_kron_one_mul, MIPRE.qform_extVecA,
  MIPRE.stateSqNorm_sub_of_isometry, MIPRE.conjTranspose_mul_self_of_involution,
  MIPRE.exists_exactly_linear_close}
\leanok
Let $\{O^u\}_{u \in V}$, $V = \F_q^n$, be observables on the first party's space --- self-adjoint
involutions --- and let $\ket{\psi}$ be a state. Then, on the space extended by an ancilla register
$\C^V$ on the \emph{first party only}, with the extended state
$\ket{\psi'} = \ket{\psi} \ot \ket{0}$, there are observables $\{L^u\}$ that are \emph{exactly}
linear,
\begin{equation*}
  L^u L^{u'} \;=\; L^{u + u'}\;, \qquad L^0 \;=\; \Id\;,
\end{equation*}
such that the average squared distance to the original family is \emph{equal} to the average
linearity defect:
\begin{equation*}
  \E_{u \in V} \norm{\bigl(L^u - O^u \ot \Id\bigr) \ket{\psi'}}^2
    \;=\; \E_{a, b \in V} \norm{\bigl(O^a O^b - O^{a + b}\bigr) \ket{\psi}}^2\;.
\end{equation*}
In particular, if the family is $\delta$-approximately linear on average then
$L^u \approx_\delta O^u$ on average over $u$: the error is linear in $\delta$, with no loss in the
exponent and no change of constant.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{def:generalized-pauli,lem:naimark-dilation}
The Fourier argument, in three exact steps and one identity.

\textbf{Parseval.} Let $A_e = \abs{V}^{-1} \sum_a (-1)^{\fieldtr(a \cdot e)} O^a$ be the Fourier
transform of the family against the characters of the trace form
(Definition~\ref{def:generalized-pauli}). Merging the characters and summing over $e$ collapses the
double sum, because $\sum_e (-1)^{\fieldtr(c \cdot e)}$ is $\abs{V}$ at $c = 0$ and $0$ otherwise:
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:linearity-character-sum}
  \sum_e (-1)^{\fieldtr(u \cdot e)} A_e^2 \;=\; \abs{V}^{-1} \sum_a O^a O^{a + u}\;.
\end{equation}
At $u = 0$ the right-hand side is $\abs{V}^{-1} \sum_a (O^a)^2 = \Id$, so $\{A_e^2\}$ is a POVM ---
\emph{exactly}, with no appeal to the approximate linearity. That is the only place the involution
property is used.

\textbf{Naimark.} Dilate that POVM to a projective measurement $\{P_e\}$ on
$\C^{d} \ot \C^{V}$, compressed back by the isometry $\ket{v} \mapsto \ket{v} \ot \ket{0}$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:naimark-dilation}). One isometry serves the whole family of families, which is what
lets the estimate be taken against the single state $\ket{\psi'}$.

\textbf{Inversion.} Put $L^u = \sum_e (-1)^{\fieldtr(u \cdot e)} P_e$. Mutual orthogonality of the
$P_e$ gives $L^u L^{u'} = L^{u + u'}$ and $L^0 = \sum_e P_e = \Id$, and the compression of $L^u$ is
the left-hand side of~\eqref{eq:linearity-character-sum}, hence $\abs{V}^{-1} \sum_a O^a O^{a+u}$.

\textbf{The identity.} Expanding both squared distances --- each is $2$ minus the two orders of an
overlap, the operators involved being unitary --- and compressing, the $u$-th term on the left is
the average over $a$ of the $(a, a+u)$-th term on the right, after reindexing that average by
$a \mapsto a + u$. Summing over $u$ and reindexing once more, by $u \mapsto a + u$ for each fixed
$a$, gives the stated equality. No inequality is used anywhere, which is why the constant is
exactly $1$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Source and correction.} The paper imports this from Natarajan and
Vidick~\cite{NV17} (Theorem~10 of the arXiv version) and proves nothing about it; the source is
vendored in the \emph{paper} repository, not in this one. An earlier version of this proof text said
``the source is vendored and was audited line by line'', which is true of
\texttt{paper/external/nv17/} and false of \texttt{MIPRE/Background/}, whose vendored trees are
GowersHatami, LIDT, Orthonormalization and Repetition. A reader would have planned the wrong work.
The theorem is therefore \textbf{proved here}, from Parseval and Lemma~\ref{lem:naimark-dilation};
it is formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/Linearity.lean}.

The ledger node this statement accounts for records the \emph{paper}'s status --- imported from
NV17, with the import audited in its three children: the statement match, the source's
state-dependent-distance toolkit, and the Fourier proof line by line. The mark here records the
\emph{Lean}'s status, which is a proof. The two are consistent claims about different artifacts, and
the Lean proof is the argument the third child audits: the same transform, the same Parseval step,
the same dilation, the same inversion.

\paragraph{Comments.} Three things are worth separating from the proof.

Theorem~\ref{thm:gowers-hatami} is \emph{not} used, and has left this theorem's \texttt{uses} list.
Gowers--Hatami is a statement about the Hilbert--Schmidt distance and approximate
\emph{representations}; the hypothesis here is in the state-dependent distance, and the
exact-POVM step above replaces the stability machinery entirely. The two results are about
different things and neither implies the other in the form stated.

The conclusion is an \textbf{equality}, which is more than the paper's statement and more than the
application needs. The paper's own audit note reconciles its $\delta$ against NV17's convention and
concludes ``the closeness exponent is $1$, with the identical $\delta$''; the equality above says
where that comes from --- the linearity defect is transported term by term, not estimated.

The ancilla is adjoined to \textbf{one} party. That is the asymmetric extension
$\ket{\psi} \ot \ket{0}_{\alice'}$ of the source statement, not the two-sided
$\ket{\EPR_q}^{\ot M}$ of Definition~\ref{def:expanded-state}; the exactly linear observables act
on $\alice \alice'$ and the second party is untouched. Its formalization is \texttt{extVecA}, with
the compression identity \texttt{qform\_extVecA} the only fact about it the proof needs.

\effortHard

In the remaining combining and separating obligations, cross-party
consistency estimates use the outcome-summed squared distance of
Definition~\ref{def:cross-distance}. When a proof instead bounds the
probability of unequal outcomes, convert using
Equation~\ref{eq:cross-from-agreement} and retain the resulting constant.
Same-side operator comparisons use Definition~\ref{def:state-distance}.

\begin{lemma}[Combined $XZ$ point measurements]
\label{lem:qld-combined-points}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.8}
\uses{lem:qld-expanded-points,lem:naimark-dilation,def:expanded-state,def:distance}
\lean{MIPRE.fourierVec, MIPRE.sum_norm_fourierVec_sq, MIPRE.trObs, MIPRE.trFourier,

  MIPRE.swapVec_expVec, MIPRE.Weyl.swapVec_epr, MIPRE.bornProb_swapVec,
  MIPRE.povmValue_swapVec_of_symm, MIPRE.sum_weighted_mul_le_sqrt,
  MIPRE.sum_weighted_sqrt_le, MIPRE.abs_qform_conjTranspose_mul_le, MIPRE.proj_le_one,
  MIPRE.snorm_le_one_of_proj, MIPRE.bornProb_eq_qform, MIPRE.abs_qform_aOp_mul_bOp_le,
  MIPRE.stateNorm_mul_le, MIPRE.norm_stateVecB_mul_le, MIPRE.xSqNorm_eq_expand,
  MIPRE.one_sub_sum_bornProb_eq, MIPRE.sand, MIPRE.sum_sand, MIPRE.sandPOVM,
  MIPRE.sum_stateSqNorm_ord, MIPRE.abs_link1_le, MIPRE.abs_link2_le, MIPRE.abs_link3_le,
  MIPRE.link4_eq, MIPRE.link5_eq, MIPRE.one_sub_sum_bornProb_sand_le, MIPRE.extVec2,
  MIPRE.extVec2_unit, MIPRE.bornProb_extVec2, MIPRE.sum_xSqNorm_dilated_eq,
  MIPRE.sum_xSqNorm_dilated_aOp_le, MIPRE.exists_projective_joint,
  MIPRE.QLD.pairTest_symm, MIPRE.QLD.accepts_symm, MIPRE.QLD.qldGame_mu_symm,
  MIPRE.QLD.povmValue_qldGame_swapVec, MIPRE.QLD.hatVec_swapVec, MIPRE.QLD.hatPtPOVM,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatPOVM_eq, MIPRE.QLD.hatMats, MIPRE.QLD.hatObsAt, MIPRE.QLD.hatObsAt_eq,
  MIPRE.QLD.trObs_ptVal, MIPRE.QLD.trObs_synPOVM, MIPRE.QLD.hatObsAt_self,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatComm, MIPRE.QLD.hatComm_eq_fourierOf, MIPRE.QLD.sum_stateSqNorm_hatComm,
  MIPRE.QLD.isPVM_hatMats, MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_split, MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_avg_probe,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_hatComm_le, MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_hatComm_le_B,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_hatMats_consistency, MIPRE.QLD.deltaQ, MIPRE.QLD.combined_points}
\leanok
Let $\ket{\hat\psi}$ be as in Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-points}, and suppose both players'
measurements are projective --- without loss of generality, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:naimark-dilation}. Put
\begin{equation*}
  \delta_Q(\eps) \;=\; 2\sqrt{57676416\,\eps} \;+\; \sqrt{86\,\eps} \;+\; 86\,\eps
  \;=\; \poly(\eps)\;.
\end{equation*}
Then for every pair $(x, z) \in \F_q^m \times \F_q^m$ and each player there is a
\emph{projective} measurement $\{\widehat{Q}^{x, z}_{a, b}\}_{a, b \in \F_q}$, acting on that
player's expanded space enlarged by one $\F_q \times \F_q$ ancilla register held in the state
$\ket{0, 0}$, which jointly returns the $X$-value at $x$ and the $Z$-value at $z$ and satisfies,
on average over the verifier's content:
\begin{enumerate}
\item (\textbf{Self-consistency})
  $\widehat{Q}^{x, z}_{a, b} \ot \Id \;\simeq_{2 \delta_Q}\; \Id \ot \widehat{Q}^{x, z}_{a, b}$;
\item (\textbf{Consistency with the ordered products})
  \begin{gather*}
    \widehat{Q}^{x, z}_{a, b} \ot \Id \;\simeq_{4 \delta_Q + 115352832 \eps}\;
      \Id \ot \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, Z), z}_b \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, X), x}_a\;, \\
    \widehat{Q}^{x, z}_{a, b} \ot \Id \;\simeq_{4 \delta_Q + 461411328 \eps}\;
      \Id \ot \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, X), x}_a \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, Z), z}_b\;.
  \end{gather*}
\end{enumerate}
Moreover $\widehat{Q}^{x, z}$ compresses, along the isometry that adjoins $\ket{0, 0}$, to the
\textbf{sandwich}
$\widehat{R}^{x, z}_{a, b} = \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, Z), z}_b
\widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, X), x}_a \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, Z), z}_b$. The conclusions
depend on the content only through $(x, z)$, whose marginal is uniform, so ``on average over the
content'' is ``on average over uniform $(x, z)$''. The marginals of $\widehat{Q}^{x,z}$ are not
claimed here; they are obtained in Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-pairs-of-lines}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-expanded-points,lem:naimark-dilation,def:expanded-state,def:distance,
  def:generalized-pauli}
The sandwich, its Naimark dilation, and one five-link chain.

\textbf{The sandwich is a POVM, exactly.} With $\{\widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, Z), z}_b\}$
projective,
\begin{equation*}
  \sum_{a, b} \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b \widehat{M}^{X, x}_a \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b
    \;=\; \sum_b \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b \Bigl(\sum_a \widehat{M}^{X, x}_a\Bigr)
      \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b
    \;=\; \sum_b \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b \;=\; \Id\;,
\end{equation*}
and each term is $(\widehat{M}^{X, x}_a \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b)^\dagger
(\widehat{M}^{X, x}_a \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b)$, hence positive. No approximate commutation is used
here, and no orthonormalization is needed afterwards: Lemma~\ref{lem:naimark-dilation} dilates
$\{\widehat{R}^{x, z}_{a, b}\}$ to a \emph{projective} measurement on one extra
$\F_q \times \F_q$ register, compressed back by the isometry that adjoins $\ket{0, 0}$, and one
isometry serves every $(x, z)$ at once. Projectivity of $\widehat{M}$ itself is two closure
properties of projective measurements --- products and coarse-grainings --- applied to the
convolution of Definition~\ref{def:expanded-state}.

\textbf{Parseval moves the commutation input to the elements.} What
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-points} bounds is the commutator of the \emph{observables}
$\widehat{W}^{r}(u)$, one pair per probe; what the sandwich needs is the commutator of the
measurement \emph{elements}, one pair per outcome. The elements are the two-variable Fourier
transform of the observables against the characters of the trace form
(Definition~\ref{def:generalized-pauli}), so by Parseval the sum over outcomes of the one is the
average over probes of the other --- and the content already averages over the probes uniformly
and independently of everything else it carries, so that average is free.

\textbf{The chain.} Write $P_b = \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b \ot \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b$ and
$\Delta = \sum_a (\Id - \widehat{M}^{X, x}_a) \ot \widehat{M}^{X, x}_a$. Then
\begin{align*}
  \sum_{a, b} \bra{\hat\psi} \widehat{R}^{x, z}_{a, b} \ot \widehat{R}^{x, z}_{a, b}
      \ket{\hat\psi}
  &\approx \sum_{a, b} \bra{\hat\psi} \widehat{M}^{Z}_b \widehat{M}^{X}_a \ot
      \widehat{R}^{x, z}_{a, b} \ket{\hat\psi}
  \approx \sum_{a, b} \bra{\hat\psi} \widehat{M}^{Z}_b \widehat{M}^{X}_a \ot
      \widehat{M}^{Z}_b \widehat{M}^{X}_a \ket{\hat\psi} \\
  &\approx \sum_{a, b} \bra{\hat\psi} \widehat{M}^{Z}_b \ot
      \widehat{M}^{Z}_b \widehat{M}^{X}_a \ket{\hat\psi}
  = \sum_b \bra{\hat\psi} P_b \ket{\hat\psi}
  \;=\; 1 - \tfrac12 \sum_b \delta^Z_b\;,
\end{align*}
where $\delta^Z_b$ is the $Z$-consistency defect. The first two steps are the two players'
commutators: each difference is $\widehat{M}^Z_b$ times a commutator, the front factor is a
contraction, and one Cauchy--Schwarz over \emph{all} outcome pairs pays for it --- against
$\sum_{a,b} \norm{\widehat{R}_{a,b} \ket{\hat\psi}}^2 \leq 1$ for the first and
$\sum_{a,b} \norm{\widehat{M}^X_a \widehat{M}^Z_b \ket{\hat\psi}}^2 = 1$ for the second. The
third step is the one place the outcome sum must \emph{not} be broken up: the whole double sum is
the single product $(\sum_b P_b) \Delta$, both factors are projections, and Cauchy--Schwarz gives
$\sqrt{\bra{\hat\psi} \Delta \ket{\hat\psi}} = \sqrt{\tfrac12 \sum_a \delta^X_a}$. Breaking it up
instead would cost a factor $q$, which is exactly the defect the paper's round-11 note repairs.
Both parties' commutators are available because the game is symmetric in the players
(\texttt{MIPRE.QLD.accepts\_symm}).

\textbf{Transport.} The compression identity of the dilation turns every Born-rule expectation on
the extended state into one on $\ket{\hat\psi}$, so the chain's bound is the dilated pair's
self-consistency with no further loss, and the same expansion against
$\widehat{R}^{x, z}_{a, b} \ot \Id$ gives item 2 after one triangle inequality replacing the
sandwich by each ordered product --- the difference being $\widehat{M}^Z_b$ times a commutator
again.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Source and correction.} Formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Combined.lean},
with the generic mathematics in \texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/Sandwich.lean}, the Fourier dictionary
in \texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/Parseval.lean} and the player exchange in
\texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/Swap.lean} and \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Swap.lean}.

The \textbf{route differs from the paper's}, and the statement is the same. The paper builds
$\widehat{Q}$ in three steps: the sandwich, then \emph{orthonormalization}
(Corollary~\ref{cor:ortho-from-consistency}) to make it projective, then the quantum linearity
test (Theorem~\ref{thm:linearity}) to turn the resulting binary observables into an $\F_q^2$-valued
measurement. Given that the strategy may be taken projective --- which the paper also assumes ---
the last two steps are unnecessary: the sandwich is already a POVM, so its Naimark dilation is
already the projective joint measurement wanted, and the compression identity carries the
estimates. So \texttt{thm:linearity} has left this lemma's \texttt{uses} list, together with
\texttt{cor:ortho-from-consistency}, which was never in it. Theorem~\ref{thm:linearity} is
consequently used nowhere in this blueprint --- Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-exact-paulis} says explicitly
that it is not used there either --- and is kept because it is a statement of the paper's and is
now proved rather than imported, not because anything downstream needs it.

The \textbf{extra register is explicit}. The paper writes ``by assuming that $\ket{\hat\psi}$ has
sufficiently many ancilla zero qubits, we do not need to extend the state further''; here the
$\F_q \times \F_q$ register the dilation needs is adjoined to each party by name, which is the
same device with the bookkeeping visible. It is adjoined to \emph{both} parties, unlike the
one-sided extension of Theorem~\ref{thm:linearity}, because item 1 compares the two players'
copies of the same construction.

The \textbf{constants are explicit and linear in the inputs}. $\delta_Q$ has a square root because
three of the chain's five links are Cauchy--Schwarz steps; the $57676416\eps$ under the first two
is Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-obs-commutation}'s constant, unchanged by the expansion, and the $86\eps$
under the third and in the linear term is item 1 of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}'s, halved by
projectivity. Nothing in the chain is a per-outcome estimate, so no constant depends on $q$.

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Axis degree for expanded line measurements]
\label{lem:qld-axis-degree}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.6,1.2.2.9}
\uses{lem:qld-expanded-lines,lem:qld-win}
\lean{MIPRE.LowDegree.natDegree_lineRestrict_single_le, MIPRE.QLD.DegLE.add,
  MIPRE.QLD.degLE_zero, MIPRE.QLD.degLE_padLine, MIPRE.QLD.degLE_rdLine,
  MIPRE.QLD.degLE_lineCoeffs_aline, MIPRE.QLD.degLE_hatLine_outcome,
  MIPRE.QLD.Question.defaultAns, MIPRE.QLD.fmtOk_defaultAns, MIPRE.QLD.legalize,
  MIPRE.QLD.legalize_of_fmtOk, MIPRE.QLD.legalize_of_not_fmtOk, MIPRE.QLD.fmtOk_legalize,
  MIPRE.QLD.rdVal_legalize_point, MIPRE.QLD.rdPauli_legalize_pauli,
  MIPRE.QLD.rdBit_legalize_pairB, MIPRE.QLD.rdBit_legalize_var,
  MIPRE.QLD.rdBitPair_legalize_pair, MIPRE.QLD.rdProbe_legalize_point,
  MIPRE.QLD.LegalSupport, MIPRE.QLD.legalizeStrat, MIPRE.QLD.isPVM_legalizeStrat,
  MIPRE.QLD.legalizeStrat_mats_eq_zero, MIPRE.QLD.legalSupport_legalizeStrat,
  MIPRE.QLD.legalizeStrat_map, MIPRE.QLD.ptPOVM_legalizeStrat, MIPRE.QLD.ptObs_legalizeStrat,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatPtPOVM_legalizeStrat, MIPRE.QLD.hatMats_legalizeStrat,
  MIPRE.QLD.povmValue_le_legalizeStrat, MIPRE.QLD.one_sub_povmValue_legalizeStrat_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.lineAnsPOVM_aline_eq_zero_of_not_degLE,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatLinePOVM_aline_eq_zero_of_not_degLE,

  MIPRE.QLD.padLineMats_aline_eq_zero_of_not_degLE}

Without loss of generality the strategy answers every question in the format its type
prescribes: replacing each answer of the wrong format by a fixed legal one keeps the
measurements projective, does not decrease the success probability, and leaves every
reading the rules and the conclusions use --- point values, trace probes, Pauli encodings,
bits --- unchanged. For such a strategy and $d \geq 1$, the expanded line measurement of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-lines} at an axis-parallel line is supported on polynomials of
degree at most $d$: it has no element on any other outcome. The same holds for the padded
line measurement of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-padded-lines} at an axis-parallel padded line, so
its outcomes are legal answers to the axis-parallel line questions of the seeded test at
$(q,4m,d,1)$.
The original strategy's axis-line answers have degree at most $d$ by their legal format.
Expansion convolves that outcome with the restriction of a multilinear Pauli encoding. The
latter has degree at most one on an axis line, so addition preserves the bound for $d\geq1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-expanded-lines,lem:qld-win}
\leanok
Legalizing (\texttt{legalizeStrat}) relabels each measurement along the map that keeps a
well-formatted answer and sends any other to a default legal answer reading as $0$. A
relabelling of a projective measurement is projective. The decider rejects an answer of the wrong
format before running any rule, so every accepted answer pair is left alone and the accepted
mass can only grow (\texttt{povmValue\_le\_legalizeStrat}, by data processing on the Born
distribution). Every reading of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}'s rules sends ill-formatted answers to
$0$, as the defaults do, so the point-probe, hatted point and Pauli readings of the two strategies
coincide.

For a legally supported strategy the strategy's factor of the expanded axis-line measurement is
supported on \texttt{apoly} answers, of degree at most $d$, and the ancilla's factor on
restrictions of the multilinear encoding to the axis line, of degree at most $1$
(\texttt{natDegree\_lineRestrict\_single\_le}); their convolution is supported on degree at most
$d$ for $d \geq 1$ (\texttt{hatLinePOVM\_aline\_eq\_zero\_of\_not\_degLE}). The padded line
measurement is an average of sandwiches of the two sides' axis-line elements, coarse-grained by
the combining map, whose degree bound \texttt{degLE\_padCombine\_aline} contraposes: an outcome
of degree above $d$ can only come from a pair with a factor of degree above $d$, and that factor
vanishes.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} \textbf{Proved, exactly and at no cost, by legalizing the strategy first.}
An earlier version of this paragraph costed the support claim as ``coarse-grain the ill-formatted
answers into a default outcome and re-derive the two items of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-lines}
for the modified measurement at the cost of the format-failure probability, which is $O(\eps)$ but
changes the constants''. That is one legalization too late. Legalizing the \emph{strategy} rather
than the expanded measurement is where the paper's own convention lives --- a ``valid strategy''
answers in the prescribed format, which is how \texttt{qld-combining.tex} justifies the axis
bound --- and it costs nothing: the value does not decrease, projectivity is kept, and the
readings are unchanged, so every lemma of this appendix applies to the legalized strategy verbatim
and its conclusions about point and Pauli measurements are conclusions about the original. The
support claim is then exact --- zero mass, not $O(\eps)$ --- and no constant moves.
\texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Legalize.lean} is the legalization; the support half sits next to
the degree computation in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/PaddedLines.lean}.

\begin{lemma}[Pairs of lines]
\label{lem:qld-pairs-of-lines}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.9}
\uses{lem:qld-combined-points,lem:qld-expanded-lines,lem:pasting-updated,
  lem:cool-closeness-fact,lem:schwartz-zippel}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.toPoly, MIPRE.QLD.natDegree_toPoly_le, MIPRE.QLD.eval_toPoly,
