  MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_shift_gen, MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_shiftAlong,
  MIPRE.QLD.dirOf_shiftPt, MIPRE.QLD.ddirOf_shiftPt, MIPRE.QLD.abaseOf_shiftAlong,
  MIPRE.QLD.dbaseOf_shiftAlong, MIPRE.QLD.question_aline_shiftAlong,
  MIPRE.QLD.question_dline_shiftAlong, MIPRE.QLD.hatLinePOVM_congr, MIPRE.QLD.LinePres,
  MIPRE.QLD.LinePres.param, MIPRE.QLD.LinePres.param_shiftAlong,
  MIPRE.QLD.LinePres.lineMats, MIPRE.QLD.LinePres.lineEvalMats,
  MIPRE.QLD.LinePres.isPVM_lineMats, MIPRE.QLD.LinePres.isPVM_lineEvalMats,
  MIPRE.QLD.LinePres.lineEvalMats_eq_fibSum, MIPRE.QLD.LinePres.lineMats_shiftAlong,
  MIPRE.QLD.LinePres.lineEvalMats_shiftPt_other, MIPRE.QLD.aPres, MIPRE.QLD.dPres,
  MIPRE.QLD.aPres_dir_ne_zero, MIPRE.QLD.collProb, MIPRE.QLD.card_collide_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_degenerate_le, MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_collProb_aPres,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_collProb_dPres, MIPRE.QLD.valOf, MIPRE.QLD.pairEquiv,
  MIPRE.QLD.extHat, MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_normSq_point_line_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_xSqNorm_marg_point_le, MIPRE.QLD.sum_xSqNorm_marg_line_le_aux,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_marg_line_le, MIPRE.QLD.pasteLine, MIPRE.QLD.fibSum_aOp,
  MIPRE.QLD.pasteJ_eq_pasteLine, MIPRE.QLD.pairs_of_lines_gen, MIPRE.QLD.pairs_of_lines,
  MIPRE.QLD.kappaPairs,
  MIPRE.QLD.deltaPairsD, MIPRE.QLD.deltaPairs, MIPRE.QLD.pairs_of_lines_of_items,
  MIPRE.QLD.aPres_items, MIPRE.QLD.dPres_items, MIPRE.QLD.qld_pairs_of_lines,
  MIPRE.xSqNorm_extVec2_aOp, MIPRE.normSq_stateVecB_sub_le}
\leanok
Let $\ket{\hat\psi}$ and $\{\widehat{Q}^{x,z}_{a,b}\}$ be as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points}. Fix a \emph{line presentation} on each side --- a line type,
axis-parallel or diagonal, so that each content presents a line $\ell_W$ with a base point and a
direction on side $W$, and the sampled point $u_W$ lies on it. For a pair of degree-$md$
polynomials $(f_X, f_Z)$ on the two lines put
\begin{equation*}
  T^{\ell_X, \ell_Z}_{f_X, f_Z}
    \;=\; \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, X), \ell_X}_{f_X}\,
      \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, Z), \ell_Z}_{f_Z}\,
      \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, X), \ell_X}_{f_X}\;,
\end{equation*}
which is a POVM with outcomes pairs of degree-$md$ polynomials. Then, on average over the
verifier's content,
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:qld-pairs-of-lines-bp}
  \widehat{Q}^{x,z}_{a,b} \ot \Id
    \;\simeq_{2 \delta_P}\; \Id \ot T^{\ell_X, \ell_Z}_{[\mathrm{eval}_x(\cdot) = a,\,
      \mathrm{eval}_z(\cdot) = b]}\;,
\end{equation}
with $\delta_P = \poly(\eps, \eps_c)$ where $\eps_c$ is the average collision probability of the
$X$-side evaluation map: $md/q$ for an axis-parallel line and $md/q + 1/q$ for a diagonal one.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-combined-points,lem:qld-expanded-lines,lem:pasting-updated,
  lem:cool-closeness-fact,lem:schwartz-zippel}
This is the pasting lemma (Lemma~\ref{lem:pasting-updated}) at
$G_1 = \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, Z), \ell_Z}$, $G_2 = \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Line}, X),
\ell_X}$ and $A = \widehat{Q}^{x,z}$, and what has to be supplied is its four hypotheses.

\textbf{The two marginal consistencies.} Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points} gives the joint
measurement against the \emph{ordered product} of the two point measurements, in either order; the
marginal step of Lemma~\ref{lem:cool-closeness-fact} turns each of those into the corresponding
marginal against a single point measurement, at ten times the input. Replacing that point
measurement by the line measurement evaluated at the point is one triangle inequality through
Bob's own point measurement --- whose two legs are item 1 of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-points}
(Alice's copy against Bob's) and item 2 of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-lines} (Alice's point
against Bob's line).

\textbf{The fine-grained self-consistency of the $X$-line measurement} is item 1 of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-lines} verbatim. The joint measurement lives on each party's space
enlarged by one $\F_q \times \F_q$ register and the line measurements do not, so both inputs are
transported by the fact that an operator with an inert ancilla has the same cross-party deviation
on the extended state as the operator itself.

\textbf{The collision term} needs the point to be uniform on its line once the line is fixed. The
content determines both, so the factorization is produced by \emph{shifting}: for a fixed
$t \in \F_q$ the map that replaces $u_W$ by $u_W + t \cdot w_W$ is a bijection of contents, so
averaging over contents and over (content, shift) pairs give the same thing; the line's question,
base point and direction are invariant under it; and the point's parameter moves by exactly $t$.
Two distinct degree-$md$ polynomials then agree at at most $md$ of the $q$ parameters, which is the
univariate case of Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Lines.lean}, on top of
Lemma~\ref{lem:pasting-updated} in \texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/Pasting.lean}. The headline
declaration \texttt{qld\_pairs\_of\_lines} takes only game-level hypotheses --- a strategy, its
projectivity, and $1 - \val \leq \eps$ --- and produces the joint measurement together
with~\eqref{eq:qld-pairs-of-lines-bp} at the axis-parallel line of each side;
\texttt{pairs\_of\_lines\_of\_items} is the same statement for any pair of presentations, and
\texttt{aPres\_items}, \texttt{dPres\_items} and \texttt{sum\_content\_collProb\_dPres} are what
the other three pairs of line types need.

\textbf{The question distribution is a parameter.} \texttt{pairs\_of\_lines\_gen} proves the lemma
over any finite nonempty sample space carrying a content per side --- $k_X$ presenting the $X$ line,
$k_Z$ the $Z$ line --- together with a shift of the $X$ point that both contents see as such; the
two contents need share nothing, because no hypothesis of Lemma~\ref{lem:pasting-updated} involves
both lines. \texttt{pairs\_of\_lines} is its instance at one content per sample, the same one on
both sides, where the two lines are therefore dependent;
\texttt{pairs\_of\_lines\_prod} (Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-padded-lines}) is its instance at a pair of
line--point data, where they are independent. \texttt{pasteLine} and
\texttt{pasteJ\_eq\_pasteLine} accordingly take the $X$ line and the $Z$ line from two contents
rather than one.

\textbf{Per pair of line types, not for the mixture.} The paper states the lemma on average over
the line--point distribution, which is an equal mixture of the axis-parallel and diagonal
branches. Here the line type is a parameter and the statement is proved for each of the four
pairs, which is \emph{stronger}: the mixture is a convex combination of the four.

\textbf{A degenerate diagonal line makes the collision probability one, and the paper's
$\eps = md/q$ does not account for it.} The seeded test's diagonal direction is
$\mathtt{zeroBelow}(\chi(s), v)$, which vanishes for a $1/q$ fraction of the contents at worst; on
such a content the ``line'' is a single point, every polynomial is evaluated at the same parameter,
and two distinct ones can agree with probability $1$. The bound above therefore carries
$md/q + 1/q$ on the diagonal side, and $\delta_P$ is still $\poly(\eps, md/q)$. See
\texttt{reports/pasting-degenerate-diagonal-line.md}.

\textbf{The shift replaces the paper's appeal to \texttt{lem:alnf} and \texttt{lem:dlnf}.} Those
say that conditioned on the line the point is uniform on it, which is what the collision bound
uses. Rather than form the conditional distribution, the formalization shifts the point along the
direction and uses that the shift is a bijection of contents for each fixed $t$
(\texttt{sum\_content\_shift\_gen}); the same device, applied to the raw direction $v$ instead of
the point, is what bounds the probability of a degenerate diagonal line.

\textbf{What the refined contract still asks for.} Three items of the contract recorded for this
lemma are not part of what is proved above, and are wanted by the lemmas that consume it: that the
outcome polynomials have degree at most $d$ on an axis-parallel component, which is
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-axis-degree} and an additional target there; and the restriction to
specified line types and direction indices, which costs the reciprocal probability of those
choices, at most $4m^2$. That the two lines be drawn \emph{independently} is no longer one of them:
\texttt{pairs\_of\_lines\_gen} leaves the question distribution free, so the padded line--point
distribution of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-padded-lines} is an instance rather than a gap. The version above fixes the line type on each side as a parameter and is
proved for each of the four pairs, so the restriction factor is not incurred; the reciprocal cost
appears only when a caller wants the statement for the mixture.

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[Padded point measurements]
\label{lem:qld-padded-points}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.10}
\uses{lem:qld-combined-points,def:generalized-pauli,def:distance}
\lean{MIPRE.sum_norm_fourierVecRaw_sq, MIPRE.trVecRaw, MIPRE.sum_norm_trVecRaw_sq,
  MIPRE.sum_norm_char_two_sq, MIPRE.sum_avg_norm_fibre_sq, MIPRE.sum_xSqNorm_map_le,
  MIPRE.sum_mulVec', MIPRE.xSqNorm_eq_norm_evec_sq, MIPRE.sum_fibre_dev,
  MIPRE.QLD.hatOrdZX, MIPRE.QLD.hatOrdXZ, MIPRE.QLD.sum_hatOrdZX, MIPRE.QLD.sum_hatOrdXZ,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_avg_xSqNorm_fibre_eq, MIPRE.QLD.padded_points}
\leanok
Write a point of the padded space $\F_q^{4m}$ as $u = (x, z, \alpha, \beta, w)$ with
$x, z \in \F_q^m$, $\alpha, \beta \in \F_q$ and $w \in \F_q^{2m - 2}$ the dummy coordinates.
With $\delta_Q$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points}, put
\begin{equation*}
  \widehat{Q}^u_c \;=\; \widehat{Q}^{x, z, \alpha, \beta}_c
    \;=\; \sum_{\substack{a, b \,:\\ \alpha a + \beta b = c}} \widehat{Q}^{x, z}_{a, b}\;,
\end{equation*}
which returns the combined value $\alpha g_X(x) + \beta g_Z(z)$ and depends on $u$ only through
$(x, z, \alpha, \beta)$ --- that is what ``independent of the dummy coordinates'' means. Then
$\{\widehat{Q}^{u}_c\}_{c \in \F_q}$ is \emph{projective}, and
\begin{enumerate}
\item (\textbf{Self-consistency}) for \emph{every} $(\alpha, \beta)$, on average over the
  content, $\widehat{Q}^{u}_c \ot \Id \simeq_{2\delta_Q} \Id \ot \widehat{Q}^{u}_c$;
\item (\textbf{Consistency with $\widehat{M}$}) on average over the content \emph{and} over
  uniform $(\alpha, \beta)$,
  \begin{gather*}
    \widehat{Q}^{u}_c \ot \Id \;\simeq_{4\delta_Q + 115352832\eps}\;
      \Id \ot \sum_{\alpha a + \beta b = c} \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, Z), z}_b
        \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, X), x}_a\;, \\
    \widehat{Q}^{u}_c \ot \Id \;\simeq_{4\delta_Q + 461411328\eps}\;
      \Id \ot \sum_{\alpha a + \beta b = c} \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, X), x}_a
        \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, Z), z}_b\;.
  \end{gather*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{lem:qld-combined-points,def:generalized-pauli,def:distance}
Both items are about the same coarse-graining and they are free for opposite reasons.

\textbf{Item 1 is free because both families are projective.} The fibres
$\{(a, b) : \alpha a + \beta b = c\}$ partition $\F_q^2$, so the coarse-grained families are
projective too --- a sum of mutually orthogonal projections is a projection. For projective
families the summed cross-party deviation and the agreement probability determine each other
exactly, $1 - \sum_c \bra{\psi} A_c \ot B_c \ket{\psi} = \tfrac12 \sum_c \norm{(A_c \ot \Id -
\Id \ot B_c)\ket{\psi}}^2$, and agreement can only increase under a coarse-graining applied to
both sides. So the error is unchanged, for each $(\alpha, \beta)$ separately. Note that the
state-dependent distance has \emph{no} data-processing inequality of its own; projectivity is
what buys the two conversions.

\textbf{Item 2 is not free, and Parseval is what pays for it.} The right-hand families are
\emph{not} POVMs --- they are products of two measurements --- so the argument above does not
apply, and a per-fibre triangle inequality would cost the fibre size $q$, which the conclusion
cannot afford. Instead, write
\begin{equation*}
  U_{a, b} \;=\; \bigl(\widehat{Q}^{x,z}_{a,b}\bigr)_{\alice\alice'}
    - \bigl(\widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b \widehat{M}^{X, x}_a\bigr)_{\bob\alice''}\;,
  \qquad
  D_c \;=\; \sum_{\alpha a + \beta b = c} U_{a, b}\;,
\end{equation*}
so that item 2's deviation is $\norm{D_c \ket{\hat\psi}}$. Parseval over $\F_q$ turns the sum
over fibres into an average over one probe,
$\sum_c \norm{D_c\ket{\hat\psi}}^2 = \E_r \norm{\sum_{a,b} (-1)^{\fieldtr(r(\alpha a + \beta
b))} U_{a,b} \ket{\hat\psi}}^2$, and two things then happen. The $r = 0$ term vanishes
\emph{identically}, because $\sum_{a,b} U_{a,b} = \Id - (\sum_a \widehat{M}^X_a)(\sum_b
\widehat{M}^Z_b) = 0$ --- completeness of all three families, and the only place the zero-sum
property is used. For $r \neq 0$ the pair $(r\alpha, r\beta)$ is uniform on $\F_q^2$ when
$(\alpha, \beta)$ is, so averaging over $(\alpha, \beta)$ and applying Parseval over $\F_q^2$
(Definition~\ref{def:generalized-pauli}) returns $\sum_{a,b}
\norm{U_{a,b}\ket{\hat\psi}}^2$ exactly. Summing the $q - 1$ nonzero probes gives
$\tfrac{q-1}{q} \sum_{a,b} \norm{U_{a,b}\ket{\hat\psi}}^2$, which is item 2 of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points} with a factor below one.

The second relation is the same argument with the two orders exchanged, using
$\sum_{a,b} \widehat{M}^{X, x}_a \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b = \Id$ in place of the other order.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Combined.lean}, with Parseval
over one and two copies of the field and the coarse-graining identity in
\texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/Parseval.lean} and \texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/Sandwich.lean}.

The error is \textbf{improved, not merely preserved}: item 2 comes out at $\tfrac{q-1}{q}$ times
the input, so the factor is below one and there is no $md/q$ term. That is the paper's
round-12 repair of this step, and the ledger node records it: an earlier
orthogonality-and-Cauchy--Schwarz route claimed $O(\delta_Q^{1/2})$ and was retracted, every
direct route paying the fibre multiplicity $q$. The Lean statement keeps the factor visible
rather than absorbing it.

Item 1 holds for \textbf{every} $(\alpha, \beta)$, not only on average, because nothing in its
proof averages over the probes --- the coarse-graining is free pointwise. Only item 2 needs the
$(\alpha,\beta)$-average, and it needs it for the bijection $(\alpha, \beta) \mapsto (r\alpha,
r\beta)$.

\effortMedium

For the seeded padded line distribution on $\F_q^{4m}$ there is a distribution
of $(\ell,\ell_X,\ell_Z)$ with the following properties. Its $\ell$-marginal
is the padded line marginal. Every $u=(u_X,u_Z,\alpha,\beta,w)\in\ell$
satisfies $u_X\in\ell_X$ and $u_Z\in\ell_Z$. For uniform $u\in\ell$, the
law of $(\ell_X,\ell_Z)$ is a mixture of products of restricted unpadded
line marginals; conditional on this pair, each of $u_X,u_Z$ is uniform on
its respective line. Joint independence of these two points is not asserted.
Here a restricted axis distribution fixes direction $e_i$; a restricted
diagonal distribution fixes the seed index $i$ and has direction with its
first $i-1$ coordinates zero. These are restrictions of the seeded laws,
including degenerate lines, not new uniform laws on distinct geometric lines.
Finally, if $\ell$ is axis-parallel, both $\ell_X$ and $\ell_Z$ are
axis-parallel. This last clause is needed for the legal degree-$d$ answers.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\leanok
\uses{def:lidt-cl}
The distribution is a sampling procedure, and the procedure is forced by the first property it
has to satisfy.

\textbf{What the containment forces.} Moving along the padded line moves the $X$ block of a point
along the $X$ block of the padded direction. So where that block is nonzero the $X$ subline has no
choice: its direction must be exactly that block, and the only freedom left is \emph{which seed}
presents it --- a seed of the prescribed axis index, drawn uniformly from that index's block of
$q/m$ elements. Where the block vanishes the $X$ block of a point is constant along the padded
line, the containment is free, and the subline is sampled fresh. Same for $Z$.

\textbf{The four cases, and why the two sides differ.} A coordinate position of
$\F_q^{4m}$ lies in the $X$ block, in the $Z$ block, at $\alpha$ or $\beta$, or among the dummy
coordinates, and which blocks of the padded direction vanish depends on the case \emph{and} on the
line type. For an axis-parallel padded line the direction is a single coordinate, so at most one of
the two blocks is nonzero. For a diagonal one it is $v$ truncated below the axis index, so an index
in the $X$ block leaves the whole $Z$ block untouched: both blocks are nonzero and the $Z$ subline
is forced as well, at axis index $0$ since nothing of its direction is truncated. That is the only
place the construction looks at the line type.

\textbf{The law.} The $X$ and $Z$ blocks of a uniform padded point are uniform and independent,
because $\F_q^{4m}$ is the disjoint union of the two blocks and the $2m$ positions above them, and
precomposition with an injection of index types carries the uniform measure to the uniform measure.
The two sides' data are therefore functions of disjoint parts of the randomness: the point and the
raw direction contribute one block each, and each side's fresh seed is its own. Averaging over the
sampling space is then exactly averaging over a product of two line-point laws, restricted on a side
precisely when that side is forced, with no constant left over --- the multiplicities are the
assignments the construction does not read, and they divide out. A side that is not forced gets the
unrestricted law, which is the uniform mixture of the $m$ restricted ones; that is the mixture in
the second property.

\textbf{The third property is definitional here}, the line type being a parameter handed to both
sides rather than something the construction chooses. What the paper draws from its Property~3 ---
that the directions agree --- is the four-case statement above.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Formalized in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Padded.lean}, on the block
identities of \texttt{MIPRE/Foundations/Blocks.lean}.

\textbf{The construction.} \texttt{LPData} is the triple $(u, s, v)$ a seeded line--point pair is
generated from, in either dimension; \texttt{subX} and \texttt{subZ} are the sampling procedure,
reading the two $m$-dimensional pairs off a padded one together with a fresh seed and raw direction
per side. \texttt{padCase} is the four-way case distinction, and \texttt{seedIn} is the retargeting
of a seed to a prescribed block keeping its offset --- the paper's ``choose $s_X$ uniformly at
random subject to $\chi(s_X) = i$'', made a function so that the construction is a function of its
randomness rather than a conditional distribution.

\textbf{The containment} is \texttt{xBlk\_dir\_sub} and \texttt{zBlk\_dir\_sub}, which say that in
each case the relevant block of the padded direction either \emph{is} the subline's direction or
vanishes, and \texttt{xBlk\_on\_subX} / \texttt{zBlk\_on\_subZ}, which turn that into
$u_X \in \ell_X$ and $u_Z \in \ell_Z$.

\textbf{The law is stated as four identities, one per case}, and they are identities of averages
rather than of sums: \texttt{avgSub\_free}, \texttt{avgSub\_zc}, \texttt{avgSub\_xc} and
\texttt{avgSub\_xc\_dline} each say that the average over the sampling space \emph{equals} the
average over a product of two line--point laws, restricted on a side exactly when that side is
forced. No constant survives; the multiplicities --- the assignments to the coordinates and the
fresh randomness the construction does not read --- are what the normalization divides out, and
they differ from case to case, which is why the four are separate statements.
\texttt{sumAll\_eq\_sum\_sumRestr} is the mixture: the unrestricted law is the sum of the $m$
restricted ones, so a side that is not forced contributes a uniform mixture.

\textbf{Property 1 is definitional and Property 3 is definitional}, for the same reason: the padded
line--point pair is an input the construction does not modify, and the line type is a parameter
handed to both sides. \texttt{subX\_question\_aline} and \texttt{subZ\_question\_aline} record the
second of these.

\textbf{Why $4m$.} $\alpha$ and $\beta$ sit at positions $2m$ and $2m+1$, so the dummy block is the
$2m-2$ positions above them and every index bound needs only $m \geq 1$. The asymmetry between the
two sides in the diagonal case is what the paper's round-12 note on this lemma repairs: the missing
primes on $w_X, w_Z$, and the unspecified law of the coordinate registers $s_X, s_Z$.

\effortHard

For every padded line $\ell$, there is a POVM with polynomial outcomes of
degree at most $md+1$, and --- given Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-axis-degree} --- at
most $d$ when $\ell$ is axis-parallel.
On the padded line--point distribution it is consistent with the opposite
padded point measurement, with error
$\delta_{\mathrm{combine}}=\poly(m)\cdot\poly(\eps,md/q)$, in both register
versions.
The answer is the restriction of $\alpha f_X(x)+\beta f_Z(z)$.
On an axis line, either an $x$ or $z$ coordinate varies with constant
$\alpha,\beta$, or only one coefficient varies, giving degree at most $d$
for $d\geq1$. The general bound $md+1\leq4md$ and the separate axis bound
make these legal answers for the seeded test at $(q,4m,d,1)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-pairs-of-lines,lem:qld-sublines,lem:qld-padded-points,lem:qld-axis-degree}
\leanok
The measurement is the conditional average, over the sublines the construction of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-sublines} draws for the given padded line, of the pasted line measurement
coarse-grained by the combining map; its outcome at a point of $\ell$ is
$\alpha f_X(x) + \beta f_Z(z)$ by construction, and the degree bounds are the ones the Comments
below record.

For the consistency, the joint law of the two sublines-with-points is, at a fixed padded seed,
\emph{exactly} a product of the two marginal laws, each restricted or not according to which block
the padded axis index lies in. So Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-pairs-of-lines} on a product of two independent
line--point laws applies directly, and the padded law is at most $m^2$ times the product of the two
unrestricted laws. Coarse-graining the outcome pair $(f_X(x),f_Z(z))$ along
$(r_1,r_2)\mapsto \alpha r_1 + \beta r_2$ only increases agreement, and $\alpha$ and $\beta$ at the
sampled point are uniform and independent of both sublines, because translating the padded point in
those two coordinates alone leaves both sublines fixed. Chaining the three gives
$\delta_{\mathrm{combine}} = m^2\,\delta_P$. The other register version is this one applied to the
swapped strategy on the swapped state.

The paper instead chains three Cauchy--Schwarz claims, because it takes the joint law to be
correlated; that is so given the padded \emph{line}, but the quantity conditions on the two
\emph{sublines}, which do not determine the relative offset of the two blocks. See
\texttt{reports/padded-lines-product-law.md}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The statement and its proof are formalized; the consistency bound
$\delta_{\mathrm{combine}}$ was the last piece to close. Everything the statement asserts \emph{about the measurement} is formalized ---
the combining map, its two degree bounds, the POVM $\widehat Q^\ell$ itself and the
coarse-graining that turns it into an $\F_q$-valued outcome --- in
\texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Combine.lean}, on top of the transfer in
\texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Product.lean}. The transfer is worth separating from the rest because it
is where an apparent obstruction turns out not to be one.

\textbf{The obstruction, and why it is not one.} Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-pairs-of-lines} is proved on the
Pauli basis test's own question distribution: one content, carrying both sides' points but a
\emph{single} seed and a \emph{single} raw direction, so the $X$ line and the $Z$ line it presents
are not independent. This lemma needs the same conclusion on a \emph{product} of two independent
line--point laws, which is what Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-sublines} delivers. The two distributions are
genuinely different --- but \textbf{no hypothesis of the pasting lemma involves both lines}. Each
marginal consistency involves one line and the two points; the fine self-consistency and the
collision term involve one line only. So the only laws that have to agree are one side's line--point
data on its own, one side's data together with the \emph{other side's point}, and the two points; and
each of those has the same law under a content as under a product, because a content's two points and
its seed and raw direction are independent and uniform. \texttt{avg\_content\_eq\_pair\_X},
\texttt{avg\_content\_eq\_pair\_Z} and \texttt{avg\_content\_eq\_pair\_pts} are those three
identities, each one line on top of \texttt{avg\_comp\_equiv\_fst}: exhibit the splitting of each
sample space that isolates the part the quantity depends on, and the marginals match.

\textbf{The change of variables generalizes for free.} The device that makes the point uniform on its
line without forming a conditional distribution needs a finite nonempty sample space, a shift action,
and a direction the shift does not move --- nothing about contents. \texttt{bijective\_shift} and
\texttt{sum\_shift\_gen} are that statement, and \texttt{sum\_pair\_shift} is its instance at a pair
with the shift acting on the $X$ side only.

\textbf{The combining map, and where its whole geometry sits.} \texttt{combine} is built through
Mathlib's \texttt{Polynomial} and truncated back to a coefficient vector: it is
$\alpha(t) f_X(x(t)) + \beta(t) f_Z(z(t))$ with all four substitutions \emph{affine} in the padded
parameter $t$, so the degree bound $md+1$ is a product of degrees and nothing more.
\texttt{eval\_padCombine} is the paper's parametrization-free defining property: at the padded
parameter $t$ the combined polynomial takes the value $\alpha \cdot f_X(x) + \beta \cdot f_Z(z)$
at the corresponding point, each side's polynomial read at the parameter of its \emph{own} subline.

The only geometric input is \texttt{xBlk\_dir\_sub} and its $Z$ twin
(Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-sublines}): the $X$ block of the padded direction is \emph{either} the $X$
subline's own direction \emph{or} zero. In the first case the subline parameter moves by exactly the
padded shift, in the second it does not move at all --- so in both it is $p_0 + c\,t$ with $c$ one or
zero, and \texttt{subAff} is that pair. The paper's case analysis over the branches of the subline
construction is not needed: the disjunction is proved once and used once.

\textbf{The axis bound is the same case distinction \texttt{padCase} already makes.} A padded
direction in the $X$ or $Z$ block leaves $\alpha$ and $\beta$ constant along the line, so the
combined polynomial inherits the degree of $f_X$ and $f_Z$ --- degree $d$ exactly when
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-axis-degree} supplies it, which is why
\texttt{degLE\_padCombine\_aline} takes that as a hypothesis. A direction at $\alpha$, $\beta$ or
a dummy coordinate leaves \emph{both} blocks constant, so the combined polynomial is affine, and
there $d \geq 1$ is all that is used.

\textbf{The measurement.} \texttt{padLineMats} is $\widehat Q^\ell$: the uniform average, over
the fresh randomness the subline construction draws, of the pasted line measurement
$T^{\ell_X,\ell_Z}$ coarse-grained by \texttt{padCombine}. Conditioning on the padded line is
exactly averaging over that randomness and nothing else, so the paper's conditional expectation is a
plain uniform average. \texttt{sum\_padLineMats} and \texttt{posSemidef\_padLineMats} are that it
is a POVM, and \texttt{sum\_filter\_padLineMats} is the data-processing step the paper's proof ends
with, in the form \texttt{pairs\_of\_lines\_prod} applies to.

\textbf{Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-pairs-of-lines}, on the product.}
\texttt{pairs\_of\_lines\_prod} is that lemma at $k_X = \mathtt{pairCX}$,
$k_Z = \mathtt{pairCZ}$ and \texttt{pairShift} as the shift: each side's content is its own point,
seed and raw direction, with the \emph{other} side's point filled in as the content's other point,
which no quantity of that side's line depends on. The two $\mathtt{rfl}$s
\texttt{pairCX\_pairShift} and \texttt{pairCZ\_pairShift} are the whole of the instantiation ---
the generalization it instantiates is recorded under
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-pairs-of-lines}, since that is the lemma it proves.

\textbf{And from the game.} \texttt{pairs\_of\_lines\_prod\_of\_items} takes only game-level
hypotheses and delivers the product form, which is what the consistency chain consumes. Two things
had to be arranged for it. First, the combined point measurement is re-indexed by the \emph{pair of
points} (\texttt{combined\_points\_pts}) rather than by the content: all four inputs of the
joint-measurement construction see the content only through its two points, so the same construction
runs at the pair, and the resulting measurement is usable at any sample space carrying two points ---
whereas the content-indexed one would have to be fed contents with zeroed probes, whose average is
not the content average. Second, \texttt{FactorsX} and \texttt{FactorsZ} say that a line
presentation reads the content only through that side's line-point data; both presentations satisfy
them by $\mathtt{rfl}$, and that is what lets \texttt{avg\_content\_eq\_pair\_X} carry each
content-level bound to the product (\texttt{avg\_pair\_eq\_content\_X} and its three
companions).

\textbf{The restricted laws, and where the factors of $m$ come from.} \texttt{avgRestr} was already
the paper's $D_{\mathrm{ty},i}$. \texttt{sum\_avgRestr} says the $m$ restricted laws average to the
unrestricted one, so each is at most $m$ times it (\texttt{avgRestr\_le\_mul\_avgAll}) and a
product of two is at most $m^2$ times the product
(\texttt{avgRestr\_prod\_le\_mul\_avgAll}). The paper's factor is $2m$ because it mixes over the
two line types; here the type is a parameter, so it is $m$. This is the whole source of the leading
$m$ in $\delta_{\mathrm{combine}}$.

\textbf{The paper's three Cauchy--Schwarz claims are avoidable here, and this is a finding.} The
paper proves the consistency by chaining Claims 17-1, 17-2 and 17-3, and says in a comment that the
chain is needed because ``under the distribution $D$ the pair is correlated (for diagonal lines both
coordinates share the line parameter), so that decomposition is not available''. The correlation is
real \emph{given the padded line} --- a uniform point of it moves both blocks with one parameter ---
but the quantity to be bounded conditions on the two \emph{sublines}, which know each block's coset
in its own space and not the relative offset of the two blocks. Marginalizing that out restores
independence, and \texttt{avgSub\_free} and its three companions
(Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-sublines}) already prove the joint law to be \emph{exactly} a product of the two
marginals at a fixed padded seed. \texttt{avgSub\_le\_mul\_avgAll} combines that with the
restricted-law transfer: the padded line--point law is at most $m^2$ times the product of the two
unrestricted subline laws, uniformly in the padded seed and the line type.
\texttt{avgSubAB\_le\_of\_forall} does the same for $\alpha$ and $\beta$ at the sampled point:
translating the padded point in those two coordinates alone is a bijection that leaves both sublines
fixed, so they are uniform and independent of everything else and a bound holding for each fixed
$(\alpha,\beta)$ holds for the joint law. No refinement of the block decomposition is needed. The
report is \texttt{reports/padded-lines-product-law.md}.

\textbf{The consistency bound is proved}, in
\texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/PaddedLines.lean}. \texttt{padded\_lines\_consistency} is it: on the
padded line--point law, at every padded seed and line type, the combined point measurement read along
$(\alpha,\beta)$ at the sampled point agrees with the pasted line measurement coarse-grained by the
combining map, up to $m^2 \cdot \delta_P$. The proof is the coarse-graining step
(\texttt{sum\_bornProb\_le\_fibre}: agreement only increases under a common coarse-graining, for
bare matrix families rather than \texttt{POVM} structures, which is what the pasted measurement is)
followed by the two domination lemmas.

\textbf{What of the statement is conditional.} Two of the three gaps this paragraph used to
list are closed. The error form was corrected to $\poly(m)\cdot\poly(\eps,md/q)$ in the statement
above, which is what is proved; and the second register version is
\texttt{padded\_lines\_consistency\_swap}, by the game's player symmetry. What remains is the
degree bound $d$ on an axis-parallel line, which is conditional on
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-axis-degree} --- an additional target there, and the reason the degree clause
is written conditionally above. So the proof mark covers the consistency bound and the measurement's
being a POVM, and the degree clause only given that lemma.
\texttt{reports/padded-lines-product-law.md} records the history, together with the size of
$\delta_P$ itself: $\delta_P \approx 7\cdot10^3\,\eps^{1/4} + 1.5\sqrt{md/q}$, so the bound is
vacuous unless $\eps \leq 4\cdot 10^{-16}$ --- a property of the whole chain's constants, not of
this step.

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[The simultaneous global measurement]
\label{lem:qld-simultaneous}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.13}
\uses{lem:qld-global-robustness}
\lean{  MIPRE.QLD.PolyPair, MIPRE.QLD.PolyPair.proj, MIPRE.QLD.evalMarg, MIPRE.QLD.evalMarg_mats,
  MIPRE.QLD.isPVM_evalMarg, MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair, MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.mono,
  MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.bornProb_aOp_aOp, MIPRE.QLD.GlobalPair.toSimulPair,
  MIPRE.QLD.exists_simulPair}
\leanok
There are universal $a>1$, $0<b<1$ and, on each relevant padded local space,
a complete projective measurement $\widehat S_{g_X,g_Z}$ of pairs of
individual-degree-$d$ polynomials on $\F_q^m$. Its evaluated $X$ and $Z$
marginals are consistent with the opposite expanded point measurements,
with error at most
$\delta_S=a(md)^a(\eps^b+q^{-b}+2^{-bmd})$.
Both register versions are required; no symmetric-strategy hypothesis is added.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-pvm,lem:qld-global-robustness}
\leanok
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-pvm} supplies the pair $(G_A, G_B)$ with error $\delta_{\mathrm{ld}}$;
Lemmas~\ref{lem:qld-global-dummy}--\ref{lem:qld-global-robustness} complete it to a projective
measurement of pairs whose evaluated marginals track the opposite party's expanded point
measurements with error $\delta_S = 2\sqrt{\Delta} + 2/q + 8\Delta$,
$\Delta = 2\delta_{\mathrm{ld}} + 2 \cdot 57676416\,\eps$, and carry it to the register shape of
the structure. The displayed functional form is reached in the assembly of
Theorem~\ref{thm:qld}, where $\delta_{\mathrm{ld}}$ is the seeded soundness theorem's error and
the square root halves the exponent $b$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The conclusion is formalized as the structure \texttt{SimulPair}
(\texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Simul.lean}): for a strategy $(\psi, M_A, M_B)$ and an error
$\delta$, ancilla types $E_A$, $E_B$, a unit state $\Phi$ on
$((d_A \times \mathrm{Anc}) \times E_A) \times ((d_B \times \mathrm{Anc}) \times E_B)$ that
reduces to the expanded state on the two hat registers, projective pair measurements $S_A$, $S_B$
with outcomes in pairs of individual-degree-$d$ polynomials, and the two evaluated-marginal
consistencies. The lemma is the existence of a \texttt{SimulPair} at error $\delta_S$
(\texttt{exists\_simulPair}); stage 5 is proved against the structure. Two departures from the
paper's statement. The sandwiched joint estimate it also announces is not carried in the
structure: stage 5 consumes only the two evaluated marginals, and the sandwich is what
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-sandwich} passes through on the way to them. And the hypothesis is
$48md \leq q$ rather than the standing $16md \leq q$, which is what makes the factor $1 - 2\eta$
of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-separate} at least $1/2$; as with $16md > q$, the regime
$48md > q$ is absorbed in the assembly by enlarging the universal constant.

Assume $m, d \geq 1$, $m \mid q$ and $4m \mid q$, and let $(\psi, M_A, M_B)$ be a projective
strategy for the Pauli basis test whose answers are in the format its questions prescribe

$\F_q^{4m}$ as $u = (x, z, \alpha, \beta, w)$ with $2m - 2$ dummy coordinates $w$. The
\emph{padded strategy} is a strategy for the seeded low individual degree test
(Definition~\ref{def:lidt-cl}) at parameters $(q, 4m, d, 1)$, played on the padded state
$\hat{\psi}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-padded-lines}. Each player answers a point question $u$ with
the sandwich $\widehat{M}^{X, x}_a \widehat{M}^{Z, z}_b \widehat{M}^{X, x}_a$ of their expanded
point measurements, coarse-grained along $(a, b) \mapsto \alpha a + \beta b$ and extended by the
identity to the combining coefficients' register --- a measurement that ignores $w$ --- and a
line question with the padded line measurement of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-padded-lines} at the
canonical base point of the line, averaged over the raw directions that produce the question, its
polynomial outcome resized to the format of the question. Every answer has the format the decider
expects, and on an axis-parallel line the resizing loses nothing. Both measurements are POVMs; the
dilation to a projective strategy is performed once, when the soundness theorem is applied
(Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-pvm}). Moving the base point of a line question to the sampled point
along the line does not change the padded line measurement, so what the decider reads is the
padded line measurement at the sample's own data, coarse-grained by evaluation at the parameter
of the sample's point --- the quantity Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-padded-lines} controls.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-padded-lines,lem:qld-padded-points,lem:qld-axis-degree,lem:qld-sublines}
\leanok
Definitions, and two invariances. The pasted line measurement is unchanged by moving the padded
point along the padded line, because each block of the point either moves along its own subline
or does not move at all (Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-sublines}); and the resizing of an axis-line answer to
degree $d$ is invisible on the support of a legally supported strategy
(Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-axis-degree}), while the resizing of a diagonal answer to degree $4md$ is a
genuine padding.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The ledger node dilates the line POVMs to projective measurements at this
point, ``using fresh local product ancillas''. The formalization keeps the strategy as a family
of POVMs and dilates once, at the application of the soundness theorem, through the seeded
soundness for POVM strategies (\texttt{MIPRE.LIDT.CL.clSoundness\_ldc\_one\_deltaCL\_of\_povm}):
on the twice-extended state the Born rule sees only compressions, so nothing has to be proved
about the dilation beyond Naimark's theorem. The node's ``$16md > q$'' clause belongs to the
assembly of Theorem~\ref{thm:qld}, where the trivial bound is used instead of the padded theorem.

In the setting of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-setup}, if the strategy wins the Pauli basis test
with probability at least $1 - \eps$, $\eps \geq 0$, then the padded strategy fails the seeded
test at $(q, 4m, d, 1)$ with probability at most
\begin{equation*}
  5\, m^2 \delta_P(\eps, md/q + 1/q) + 4\, \delta_Q(\eps) + \frac{md + 1}{q}\;,
\end{equation*}
with $\delta_P$ the error of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-pairs-of-lines} and $\delta_Q$ that of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points}; that is, $\delta' = C(\delta_{\mathrm{combine}} + \delta_Q
+ md/q)$ for a universal $C$, with $\delta_{\mathrm{combine}} = m^2 \delta_P$ as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-padded-lines}. The bound covers the line--point, identical-point and
identical-line subtests under their actual seeded distributions; line equality uses polynomial
separation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-setup,lem:qld-padded-lines,lem:qld-combined-points,lem:qld-axis-degree}
\leanok
The failure probability is the average over the verifier's samples of the conditional failure at
the two questions the sample generates, and the sample splits into the ordered pair of types and
the ambient triple (point, seed, raw direction), uniform on its own. The two cross pairs are
always accepted. Each other pair is bounded by an agreement defect of two families of POVMs
indexed by the ambient triple, averaged uniformly: a line type against a point by the agreement
of the line measurement read at the sample's point with the opposite party's padded point
measurement, which is exactly the padded consistency quantity of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-padded-lines}
once the strategy's average over raw directions is composed with the sample's; two points by the
agreement of the two padded point measurements, which is the self-consistency item of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points} read through the compressions; and two identical lines by
polynomial separation --- two distinct outcomes of degree at most $md + 1$ are told apart by
reading both at a uniformly random parameter of the line, up to $(md + 1)/q$, and the parameter
of the sample's point is uniform on the line once the raw direction is fixed and the line is not
degenerate, because translating the point along the line is a bijection of the sample space that
fixes the line and shifts the parameter. The evaluated disagreement of the two line measurements
is then bounded through the agreement triangle (the POVM version of the paper's
\texttt{fact:triangle-for-simeq}, with $11\delta$ in place of $9\delta$) by the line--point and
point--point defects. A degenerate diagonal direction has probability at most $1/q$ and is paid
for in full. Collecting the nine terms with weight $1/9$ gives the constants stated.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The decider's acceptance is only implied by, not equal to, agreement of the
relabelled answers: an ill-formatted answer that happens to agree is still rejected. The
formalization uses the implication in the one direction it holds and asks it only of answers the
strategy can produce (\texttt{condFail\_le\_one\_sub\_sum\_bornProb\_map}). The paper's proof
compares $f_A(u)$ with $f_B(u)$ at a uniformly random point $u$ of the line; the formalization
reads the sample's own point instead, which makes the uniformity of the parameter a change of
variables rather than a conditioning.

\begin{lemma}[A global padded polynomial PVM]
\label{lem:qld-global-pvm}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.13.3}
\uses{lem:qld-global-success,thm:lidt-cl-soundness-one,lem:naimark-dilation}
\lean{  MIPRE.POVM.compress_aOp, MIPRE.QLD.bornProb_extVec2_aOp_aOp, MIPRE.QLD.ansZero,
  MIPRE.QLD.PadReg, MIPRE.QLD.padState, MIPRE.QLD.padState_unit,
  MIPRE.QLD.bornProb_padState_aOp_aOp, MIPRE.QLD.padPt_aOp_mats,
  MIPRE.QLD.inconsistency_padState_aOp_right, MIPRE.QLD.inconsistency_padState_aOp_left,
  MIPRE.QLD.deltaGS, MIPRE.QLD.deltaLD, MIPRE.QLD.deltaGS_nonneg, MIPRE.QLD.exists_global_pvm,
  MIPRE.QLD.exists_global_pvm_hat, MIPRE.QLD.GlobalPair, MIPRE.QLD.exists_globalPair}
\leanok
The single-codeword theorem at $(q,4m,d,1)$, applied to a projective dilation of the padded
strategy of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-setup}, supplies projective global polynomial measurements
$G_A$, $G_B$ on the two dilated registers, with both point-consistency estimates and the
full-polynomial consistency, on the twice-padded state (the expanded state with the combining
ancillas in $\ket{0,0}$ and the dilation's answer ancillas in the zero answer). Their common error
is $\delta_{\mathrm{ld}} := \delta_{\mathrm{CL}}(q, 4m, d, \delta_{\mathrm{GS}})$, where
$\delta_{\mathrm{GS}} = 5 m^2 \delta_P(\eps, md/q + 1/q) + 4 \delta_Q(\eps) + (md+1)/q$ is the
bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-success}; it has the form
$A(md)^A((\delta_{\mathrm{GS}})^b+q^{-b}+2^{-bmd})$ for universal $A,b$. The point-consistency
estimates hold against the padded point measurements of the \emph{original} strategy --- the
expanded sandwich combinations, extended by the identity on both ancillas --- so no dilation
appears in the conclusion, and no further dilation of the projective outputs is required. The
conclusion is packaged as the structure \texttt{GlobalPair}, which the rest of stage 4 is stated
on.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-success,thm:lidt-cl-soundness-one,lem:naimark-dilation}
\leanok
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-success} is the value hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:lidt-cl-soundness-one} for the padded strategy at $(q, 4m, d, 1)$. The theorem,
in its form for POVM strategies, dilates the strategy projectively
(Lemma~\ref{lem:naimark-dilation}), adjoining to each party an ancilla indexed by the test's
answers and prepared in the zero answer, and returns projective low-degree measurements on the
dilated registers together with the three consistency estimates against the dilation's own point
measurements, on the twice-padded state. The dilation's point measurement compresses to the padded
point measurement of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-setup}. On a product state the Born probability of
an operator on one party's dilated register depends on the operator only through its compression
by the ancilla vector, so in each inconsistency the dilation's point measurement may be replaced,
term by term, by the identity extension of the padded point measurement, and the number does not
change. Expectations of doubly extended operators on the twice-padded state are their expectations
on the expanded state, so the conclusions of Lemmas~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-points}
and~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points} transfer to it.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The second Naimark dilation of the paper's proof, applied to the recovered
polynomial measurement, is not needed: the soundness theorem already returns projective
measurements (as the ledger's challenge history of node 1.2.2.13 records). The recovered
polynomials are functions on $\F_q^{4m}$, while the padded point measurements read only the
$2m+2$ coordinates $(x, z, \alpha, \beta)$; the Schwartz--Zippel argument that the polynomials do
not read the remaining dummy coordinates is Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-dummy}.

\begin{lemma}[Removing dummy-coordinate dependence]
\label{lem:qld-global-dummy}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.13.4}
\uses{lem:qld-global-pvm,lem:schwartz-zippel}
\lean{  MIPRE.LowDegree.agreeOn, MIPRE.LowDegree.mem_agreeOn,

Call an outcome $g$ of a global measurement of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-pvm} $w$-independent if
no monomial of $g$ involves a dummy coordinate: one of the $2m - 2$ coordinates of $\F_q^{4m}$
that the padded point measurements do not read. If the evaluated global measurement is
$\delta$-consistent, on average over a uniform point, with a point measurement that does not read
the dummy coordinates, then the state weight $W$ of the outcomes that are not $w$-independent
satisfies $(1 - 8md/q)\,W \leq 2\delta$; under the standing assumption $16md \leq q$,
$W \leq 4\delta$. For the measurements of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-pvm}, on either side,
$W \leq 4\delta_{\mathrm{ld}}$. The bad set is empty for $m = 1$. Retaining the other outcomes
gives a projective submeasurement.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-pvm,lem:schwartz-zippel}
\leanok
Resample the dummy coordinates: for independent uniform points $u, u'$ let $\mathrm{mix}(u, u')$
be $u$ with its dummy coordinates replaced by those of $u'$. The point measurement is the same at
$u$ and at $\mathrm{mix}(u, u')$, and $(u, u') \mapsto (\mathrm{mix}(u, u'), \mathrm{mix}(u', u))$
is an involution of the pairs, so the consistency estimate holds at both points simultaneously.
Adding the two, the operator $P_u(g(u)) + P_u(g(\mathrm{mix}(u, u')))$ is at most $2\,\Id$ when the
two values agree and at most $\Id$ when they differ, because two distinct elements of a POVM sum
to at most the identity; no projectivity of the point measurement is used. Hence
$\sum_g \langle G_g \rangle \Pr[g(u) = g(\mathrm{mix}(u, u'))] \geq 1 - 2\delta$. For a
$w$-independent outcome the probability is one. For an outcome that reads a dummy coordinate,
$g(u) - g(\mathrm{mix}(u, u'))$ is a nonzero polynomial in the $8m$ coordinates of the pair, of
individual degree at most $d$ --- its coefficient at a monomial of $g$ involving a dummy
coordinate survives --- so by Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} the probability is at most $8md/q$.
The two bounds give $(1 - 8md/q)\,W \leq 2\delta$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The paper's orthogonality step uses that the point measurements are
projective, which the dilation's are; the formalization works with the padded point measurements
of the original strategy, which are POVMs, and replaces orthogonality by
$P_b + P_{b'} \leq \Id$ for $b \neq b'$, valid for any POVM. Schwartz--Zippel is applied with
individual degrees, on two copies of the $4m$ coordinates; the formalization states
Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} for any finite variable type
(\texttt{prob\_agreeOn\_le\_individualDegree}) and bridges the seeded test's coefficient vectors
to \texttt{MvPolynomial} (\texttt{LowIndDegPoly.toMv}). The outcomes are not relabelled: the
projective submeasurement is the restriction of the global measurement to the $w$-independent
outcomes, and its complement's weight is the bound above.

\begin{lemma}[Ordered products with global outcome multipliers]
\label{lem:qld-global-products}

Let $(G_A, G_B)$ be the global measurements of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-pvm}, with error
$\delta$, on the twice-padded state $\Phi$. For an outcome $g$ put
$v_g = (G_{A,g} \otimes \Id)\Phi$, and for a padded point $u = (x, z, \alpha, \beta, w)$ let
$B_u(c)$ sum Bob's expanded ordered products $\widehat M^Z_b \widehat M^X_a$, extended by the
identity to the dilated register, over $\alpha a + \beta b = c$. Then, on average over a uniform
padded point,
$\sum_g \mathbb E_u \|v_g - (\Id \otimes B_u(g(u))) v_g\|^2 \leq 2\delta + 2\kappa$, where
$\kappa = 57676416\,\eps$ is the commutator weight of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points}. The
estimate holds for every outcome, with the product order reversed
($\widehat M^X_a \widehat M^Z_b$), and with the parties exchanged ($G_B$ against Alice's ordered
products, on the swapped state); retaining a subset of outcomes only decreases the left-hand side.
These ordered products need not be POVMs.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-pvm,lem:qld-combined-points,def:state-distance}
\leanok
Write $P_u(c) = \sum_{\alpha a + \beta b = c} \widehat M^Z_b \widehat M^X_a \widehat M^Z_b$ for
the sandwich combination the consistency of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-pvm} is stated against,
and split $\Id - B_u(c) = (\Id - P_u(c)) + (P_u(c) - B_u(c))$. Since $0 \leq P_u(c) \leq \Id$,
$(\Id - P_u(c))^2 \leq \Id - P_u(c)$, so the first part is bounded by
$\sum_g \mathbb E_u \langle G_{A,g} \otimes (\Id - P_u(g(u))) \rangle \leq \delta$. For the
second, group the outcomes by their value $c = g(u)$ and drop the projector
$\sum_{g(u) = c} G_{A,g} \leq \Id$: what remains is
$\mathbb E_u \sum_c \|(\Id \otimes (P_u(c) - B_u(c)))\Phi\|^2$, the squared norms of the fibre
sums, along $(a, b) \mapsto \alpha a + \beta b$, of the deviations
$D(a, b) = \widehat M^Z_b \widehat M^X_a \widehat M^Z_b - \widehat M^Z_b \widehat M^X_a$, which
sum to zero over $(a, b)$. Parseval over $\F_q$ turns the average over $(\alpha, \beta)$ of the
fibre sums into $(1 - 1/q) \sum_{a,b} \|(\Id \otimes D(a, b))\Phi\|^2$, with no factor $q$, and
each deviation is a contraction of a commutator:
$D(a, b) = \widehat M^Z_b [\widehat M^X_a, \widehat M^Z_b]$ for this order and
$-(\Id - \widehat M^Z_b)[\widehat M^X_a, \widehat M^Z_b]$ for the other. The commutator weight is
the one of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points}, read on the twice-padded state, where doubly
extended operators have their expanded-state norms. Bob's version applies the same argument to
the swapped state.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The paper restricts to $w$-independent outcomes and goes through the
projective joint measurement of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points}; the formalization works
with the sandwich combination itself, which is a POVM and needs no projectivity, and proves the
estimate for all outcomes, so Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-dummy} is not needed here. The two
orders are one abstract theorem (\texttt{sum\_snorm\_sq\_ordComb\_le}), which takes the identity
$\sum_{a, b} \mathrm{ord}(a, b) = \Id$ and the contraction bound as hypotheses. The error is
$2\delta_{\mathrm{ld}} + 2 \cdot 57676416\,\eps$, linear in both.

\begin{lemma}[Linearity in the two combining coefficients]
\label{lem:qld-global-linear}

\uses{lem:qld-global-pvm,lem:qld-global-products,lem:schwartz-zippel}

Let $(G_A, G_B)$ be the global measurements of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-pvm}, with error
$\delta$, on the twice-padded state $\Phi$, and let $\Delta = 2\delta + 2 \cdot 57676416\,\eps$
be the bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-products} for the order
$\widehat M^X_a \widehat M^Z_b$. Call an outcome $g$ linear in $(\alpha, \beta)$ if every
monomial of $g$ has exponents $(1, 0)$ or $(0, 1)$ on the two combining coordinates, that is
$g = \alpha g_1(x, z, w) + \beta g_2(x, z, w)$, and let $L$ be the set of such outcomes. Then,
with $\eta = (1 + 2d + 4md)/q$,
\[
  (1 - 2\eta) \sum_{g \notin L} \langle G_{A,g} \otimes \Id \rangle_\Phi \leq 2\Delta ,
\]
and likewise for $G_B$. Under $16md \leq q$ this is $O(\Delta)$, linear in $\delta$ and in
$\eps$; the sum over outcomes introduces no factor $q^2$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-pvm,lem:qld-global-products,lem:schwartz-zippel}
\leanok
Fix $g \notin L$, write $S = G_{A,g}$, and let $B_u(c)$ be the combination of the ordered
products $\widehat M^X_a \widehat M^Z_b$ along $\alpha a + \beta b = c$, for the padded point
$u = (x, z, \alpha, \beta, w)$. Since
$(S \otimes \Id)\Phi = (S \otimes B_u(g(u)))\Phi + (S \otimes (\Id - B_u(g(u))))\Phi$,
\[
  \langle S \otimes \Id \rangle
    \leq 2\,\|(S \otimes B_u(g(u)))\Phi\|^2 + 2\,\|(S \otimes (\Id - B_u(g(u))))\Phi\|^2
\]
for every $u$. Averaging over $u$ and summing over $g \notin L$, the second terms total at most
$2\Delta$, so it suffices to show $\mathbb E_u \|(S \otimes B_u(g(u)))\Phi\|^2 \leq \eta
\langle S \otimes \Id \rangle$ for each $g \notin L$.

Read the uniform padded point as a uniform base point $u_0$ with a fresh uniform pair
$(\alpha, \beta)$ substituted in the two combining coordinates. The blocks $x, z$ are those of
$u_0$, and $g(u) = p_{u_0}(\alpha, \beta)$ for the bivariate polynomial
$p_{u_0} = \sum_{i,j} g_{ij}(u_0)\,\alpha^i \beta^j$, whose coefficients $g_{ij}$ are polynomials
of individual degree at most $d$ in the remaining coordinates. As $g \notin L$, some
$g_{i_0 j_0}$ with $(i_0, j_0) \notin \{(1, 0), (0, 1)\}$ is a nonzero polynomial in $4m$
variables, so by Schwartz--Zippel it vanishes at a uniform $u_0$ with probability at most
$4md/q$; at such a base point the average over $(\alpha, \beta)$ is bounded by
$\langle S \otimes \Id \rangle$, each $B_u(c)$ being a contraction. At every other base point,
$p_{u_0}$ differs from every linear form $\alpha a + \beta b$. The pairs with $\beta = 0$
contribute at most $\langle S \otimes \Id \rangle / q$. For $\beta \neq 0$ the fibre
$\{\alpha a + \beta b = c\}$ has one $b$ per $a$, and the $\widehat M^X_a$ are orthogonal
projectors, so
$\|(S \otimes B_u(c))\Phi\|^2 = \sum_{\alpha a + \beta b = c} W(a, b)$ with
$W(a, b) = \|(S \otimes \widehat M^X_a \widehat M^Z_b)\Phi\|^2$, weights that do not depend on
$(\alpha, \beta)$ and sum to $\langle S \otimes \Id \rangle$. Exchanging the two sums, each
$W(a, b)$ is counted with the probability that $p_{u_0}(\alpha, \beta) = \alpha a + \beta b$ at a
uniform $(\alpha, \beta)$, which is at most $2d/q$ by Schwartz--Zippel in two variables.
Altogether $\eta = 1/q + 2d/q + 4md/q$. Bob's version applies the same argument to the swapped
state.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The paper (\texttt{eq:qld-g-prime-bound}) states the bound as
$O((\delta_{\mathrm{ld}} + \delta_Q)^{1/2} + md/q)$ for the $w$-independent outcomes, and
remarks that the quadratic expansion used here supports the linear bound; the formalization
proves the linear bound, for all outcomes and with an explicit constant, so
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-dummy} is not needed here. The paper goes through the projective
joint measurement of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-combined-points} and drops the $\beta = 0$ pairs at a cost
of $1/q$; here the products estimate is used directly and the $\beta = 0$ pairs are bounded by the
contraction property. The two Schwartz--Zippel applications are
\texttt{card\_eval\_eq\_zero\_le} (a nonzero coefficient vector in $4m$ variables, at $4md/q$)
and \texttt{sum\_agree\_two\_le} (two distinct bivariate coefficient vectors, at $2d/q$), both
through the bridge \texttt{LowIndDegPoly.toMv} of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-dummy}. The
coefficient vectors are \texttt{LowIndDegPoly.coef}, the coefficient of a monomial pattern on a
set of coordinates as a polynomial in the others (\texttt{eval\_eq\_sum\_coef}).

\begin{lemma}[Separating the two point variables]
\label{lem:qld-global-separate}

Let $(G_A, G_B)$, $\Phi$ and $\Delta$ be as in Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-linear}, where now
$\Delta$ bounds the estimate of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-products} for both product orders. Call
an outcome $g$ good if $g = \alpha g_X(x) + \beta g_Z(z)$ with $g_X$ reading only the $x$ block
and $g_Z$ only the $z$ block: every monomial of $g$ has exponents $(1, 0)$ on $(\alpha, \beta)$
and no other nonzero exponent outside the $x$ block, or exponents $(0, 1)$ and no other nonzero
exponent outside the $z$ block. Let $\mathrm{Good}$ be the set of good outcomes. Then, with
$\eta = (2 + 2d + 8md)/q$,
\[
  (1 - 2\eta) \sum_{g \notin \mathrm{Good}} \langle G_{A,g} \otimes \Id \rangle_\Phi
    \leq 4\Delta ,
\]
and likewise for $G_B$. In particular a good outcome reads no dummy coordinate, and the good
outcomes are in bijection with the pairs $(g_X, g_Z)$ of polynomials of individual degree at most
$d$ on $\F_q^m$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-pvm,lem:qld-global-products,lem:qld-global-linear,lem:schwartz-zippel}
\leanok
An outcome that is not good is not linear in $(\alpha, \beta)$, or is linear,
$g = \alpha g_1 + \beta g_2$, with $g_2$ reading a coordinate outside the $z$ block, or with
$g_1$ reading a coordinate outside the $x$ block. The first two kinds are handled against the
order $\widehat M^X_a \widehat M^Z_b$ and the third against $\widehat M^Z_b \widehat M^X_a$, each
by the assembly of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-linear}: it suffices to show
$\mathbb E_u \|(S \otimes B_u(g(u)))\Phi\|^2 \leq \eta \langle S \otimes \Id \rangle$ for
$S = G_{A,g}$ and each bad $g$, the non-linear ones being Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-linear}
itself. Since $1 - 2\eta$ may be taken nonnegative, the three weights add.

Fix $g = \alpha g_1 + \beta g_2$ linear with $g_2$ reading a coordinate outside the $z$ block,
and a base point $u_0$; then $g(u_0[\alpha, \beta]) = \alpha g_1(u_0) + \beta g_2(u_0)$. The
fibre $\{\alpha a + \beta b = \alpha g_1(u_0) + \beta g_2(u_0)\}$ always contains the diagonal
pair $(g_1(u_0), g_2(u_0))$, and contains any other pair for at most $q$ of the $q^2$ values of
$(\alpha, \beta)$, the equation being a nontrivial linear form in $(\alpha, \beta)$. With the
Pythagoras and weight bookkeeping of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-linear}, the average over
$(\alpha, \beta)$ is at most $2/q \cdot \langle S \otimes \Id \rangle$ plus the diagonal weight
$W(g_1(u_0), g_2(u_0)) = \langle S \otimes \widehat M^Z_{g_2} \widehat M^X_{g_1}
\widehat M^Z_{g_2} \rangle \leq \langle S \otimes \widehat M^Z_{g_2(u_0)} \rangle$. Read the
uniform $u_0$ as a uniform $z$ block with the other coordinates uniform and independent. For a
fixed $z$ block, $u \mapsto g_2(u)$ is a polynomial in the other coordinates; as $g_2$ reads a
coordinate outside the $z$ block, some coefficient of a non-constant monomial of this polynomial
is a nonzero polynomial in the $z$ block, which vanishes for at most a $4md/q$ fraction of the
$z$ blocks by Schwartz--Zippel, and where it does not vanish the polynomial is non-constant and
takes each value $b$ with probability at most $4md/q$, again by Schwartz--Zippel. So
$\mathbb E_{u_0} \langle S \otimes \widehat M^Z_{g_2(u_0)}(z) \rangle
  \leq \sum_b \mathbb E_z \Pr[g_2 = b \mid z] \langle S \otimes \widehat M^Z_b(z) \rangle
  \leq 8md/q \cdot \langle S \otimes \Id \rangle$,
using $\sum_b \widehat M^Z_b(z) = \Id$. Altogether $\eta \geq 2/q + 8md/q$. For $g_1$ reading a
coordinate outside the $x$ block, exchange the roles of $(\widehat M^X, a, \alpha)$ and
$(\widehat M^Z, b, \beta)$: the order $\widehat M^Z_b \widehat M^X_a$ along $(\alpha, \beta)$ is
the order $\widehat M^X_a \widehat M^Z_b$ along $(\beta, \alpha)$ with the two families exchanged,
and the base point is read as a uniform $x$ block. Bob's version applies the same argument to the
swapped state.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The paper states the excluded weight as
$\delta_G = O((\delta_{\mathrm{ld}} + \delta_Q)^{1/2} + md/q)$; the formalization proves the
linear bound, as for Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-linear}, and with its own constants: both
Schwartz--Zippel applications are over all $4m$ coordinates (\texttt{card\_eval\_eq\_zero\_le}),
where the paper counts $2m$ or $2m + 2$. The good outcomes exclude the dummy coordinates as well,
so this lemma subsumes Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-dummy} for the purposes of stage 4c. The
conditioning on a block is one generic statement (\texttt{sum\_uniform\_bornProb\_readOn\_le}),
for an operator family reading only the coordinates in a set $T$ and a polynomial reading a
coordinate outside $T$, with \texttt{LowIndDegPoly.restrictOff} the polynomial in the other
coordinates obtained by fixing those in $T$; the assembly is one generic statement
(\texttt{sum\_bad\_mass\_le\_of\_avg}), which Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-linear} could also use.

\begin{lemma}[Completing the pair measurement projectively]
\label{lem:qld-global-complete}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.13.8}
\uses{lem:qld-global-separate}
\lean{  MIPRE.LIDT.expandIdx, MIPRE.LIDT.expandIdx_apply_idx, MIPRE.LIDT.expandIdx_apply_of_not,
  MIPRE.LIDT.prod_pow_expandIdx, MIPRE.LIDT.LowIndDegPoly.blockPoly,
  MIPRE.LIDT.LowIndDegPoly.eval_blockPoly, MIPRE.QLD.isLinAB_of_isGood, MIPRE.QLD.notMem_xSet_iff,
  MIPRE.QLD.notMem_zSet_iff, MIPRE.QLD.not_depOutside_gA_of_isGood,
  MIPRE.QLD.not_depOutside_gB_of_isGood, MIPRE.QLD.gX, MIPRE.QLD.gZ, MIPRE.QLD.eval_gX,
  MIPRE.QLD.eval_gZ, MIPRE.QLD.pairOf, MIPRE.QLD.pairOf_of_isGood, MIPRE.QLD.pairMeas,
  MIPRE.QLD.pairMeas_mats, MIPRE.QLD.isPVM_pairMeas, MIPRE.QLD.evalMarg_pairMeas,
  MIPRE.QLD.inconsistency_map_eq}
\leanok
Relabel each good outcome $g = \alpha g_X(x) + \beta g_Z(z)$ of the global measurement by the pair
$(g_X, g_Z)$ of polynomials of individual degree at most $d$ on $\F_q^m$, and every other outcome
by the fixed pair $(0, 0)$. The result is a complete projective measurement of pairs, and the
operator it assigns to $(0, 0)$ beyond the good outcomes with that pair is the paper's complement
$R$, of weight at most $\delta_G$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-separate}. Consequently each
consistency estimate stated for the good outcomes holds for the completed measurement with its
error increased by $\delta_G$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-separate}
\leanok
The relabelling is a map on outcomes, so the completed measurement is the coarse-graining of the
global measurement by it; a coarse-graining of a projective measurement is projective, and
complete because the original is. The pair of a good outcome is read off its two coefficient
polynomials: $g_X$ is the coefficient of $\alpha$, which reads only the $x$ block, viewed as a
polynomial in $m$ variables, and $g_Z$ the coefficient of $\beta$ on the $z$ block
(\texttt{blockPoly}, whose defining property is that a coefficient vector supported on a block
evaluates through that block). Nothing is lost: for a good outcome $g_X(x) = g_1(u)$ and
$g_Z(z) = g_2(u)$ at every padded point $u$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Sandwich estimates and the two marginals]
\label{lem:qld-global-sandwich}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.13.9}
\uses{lem:qld-global-complete,lem:qld-global-products,lem:qld-global-separate,def:cross-distance}
\lean{  MIPRE.QLD.sum_uniform_zBlk, MIPRE.QLD.sum_uniform_xBlk, MIPRE.QLD.sq_sub_two_mul_le_sq,
  MIPRE.QLD.one_sub_two_sqrt_le_sum_snorm_sq, MIPRE.QLD.marg_Z_ge, MIPRE.QLD.marg_X_ge,
  MIPRE.QLD.inconsistency_evalMarg_Z_le, MIPRE.QLD.inconsistency_evalMarg_X_le}
\leanok
Let $\Delta$ be the bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-products} and $\delta_G$ the weight of the
outcomes that are not of the separated form. Then the evaluated $Z$ marginal of the completed pair
measurement is consistent with Bob's expanded $(\mathtt{Point}, Z)$ measurement at a uniform
$z \in \F_q^m$, with error at most $2\sqrt{\Delta} + 2/q + \delta_G$, and likewise the $X$
marginal with the $X$ measurement at a uniform $x$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-complete,lem:qld-global-products,lem:qld-global-separate}
\leanok
Write $B_u(c)$ for the combination of the ordered products $\widehat M^X_a \widehat M^Z_b$ along
$\alpha a + \beta b = c$. From $\sum_g \mathbb E_u \|(G_g \otimes (\Id - B_u(g(u))))\Phi\|^2 \leq
\Delta$ and $\|(G_g \otimes \Id)\Phi\| \leq \|(G_g \otimes B_u)\Phi\| +
\|(G_g \otimes (\Id - B_u))\Phi\|$, one Cauchy--Schwarz over the pairs $(u, g)$ gives
$\sum_g \mathbb E_u \|(G_g \otimes B_u(g(u)))\Phi\|^2 \geq 1 - 2\sqrt{\Delta}$. For a good
outcome, the fibre analysis of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-separate} bounds the left-hand side
above by $2/q \cdot \langle G_g \otimes \Id \rangle$ plus the diagonal weight
$\mathbb E_{u_0} \langle G_g \otimes \widehat M^Z_{g_Z(z)}(z)\rangle$, and the outcomes that are
not good contribute at most $\delta_G$ in total. The $z$ block of a uniform padded point is
uniform, so what remains is exactly the $Z$ marginal, and rearranging gives the bound. The $X$
marginal is the same computation from the other product order. No complex intermediate appears:
every quantity estimated here is a squared norm or a Born probability of a positive operator.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Global robustness and register transport]
\label{lem:qld-global-robustness}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.13.10}
\uses{lem:qld-global-sandwich,lem:qld-global-separate,lem:qld-global-setup}
\lean{  MIPRE.isPVM_reindex, MIPRE.isPVM_reindex_povm, MIPRE.reindex_aOp_aOp,
  MIPRE.POVM.aOp_aOp_reindex, MIPRE.ProjectiveMeasurement.isPVM_M, MIPRE.QLD.deltaProd,
  MIPRE.QLD.deltaSep, MIPRE.QLD.deltaS, MIPRE.QLD.GlobalPair.one_sub_two_eta_ge,
  MIPRE.QLD.GlobalPair.sum_not_isGood_mass_A_le', MIPRE.QLD.GlobalPair.sum_not_isGood_mass_B_le',
  MIPRE.QLD.liftPt, MIPRE.QLD.liftPt_mats, MIPRE.QLD.GlobalPair.inconsistency_evalMarg_A_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.GlobalPair.inconsistency_evalMarg_B_le}
\leanok
Collect the three errors of stage 4b--4c as explicit functions of the error $\delta$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-pvm} and the failure $\eps$ of the strategy:
$\Delta = 2\delta + 2 \cdot 57676416\,\eps$ is the ordered-products bound,
$\delta_G = 8\Delta$ the weight of the outcomes that are not of the separated form, and
$\delta_S = 2\sqrt{\Delta} + 2/q + \delta_G$ the marginal error. The bound
$\delta_G = 8\Delta$ holds under $48md \leq q$, where the factor $1 - 2\eta$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-separate} is at least $1/2$. Both register versions are obtained by
running the same estimates against the swapped padded state; no symmetry of the strategy is
assumed. Finally the measurements are carried from the dilated registers
$((d_A \times \mathrm{Anc}) \times \F_q^2) \times \mathrm{Ans}$ to the shape
$(d_A \times \mathrm{Anc}) \times E_A$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-simultaneous} by the associativity
reindexing, which preserves projectivity, the Born rule and every inconsistency.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-global-sandwich,lem:qld-global-separate}
\leanok
For the arithmetic: $2 + 2d + 8md \leq 12md$ for $m, d \geq 1$, so $48md \leq q$ gives
$\eta = (2 + 2d + 8md)/q \leq 1/4$ and $1 - 2\eta \geq 1/2$; since the weight is nonnegative,
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-global-separate} then reads $\delta_G \leq 8\Delta$. For the transport:
reindexing a register along an equivalence is a $*$-isomorphism of the matrix algebra, so it takes
projective measurements to projective measurements, and conjugating the state by the same
equivalence leaves every quadratic form unchanged; extending an operator by the identity twice
and reassociating is extending it once by the product ancilla. Bob's versions are Alice's applied
to the swapped state, which the inconsistency and the Born rule exchange.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Separation and the swap isometry}

\effortMedium

\begin{lemma}[The marginals track the point measurements]
\label{lem:qld-helper}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.14}
\uses{lem:qld-simultaneous,lem:qld-expanded-points,def:state-distance,def:inconsistency}
\lean{  MIPRE.stateSqNorm_eq_bornProb_one, MIPRE.normSq_stateVecB_eq_one_bornProb,
  MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.stateSqNorm_aOp, MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.normSq_stateVecB_aOp,
  MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.xSqNorm_aOp, MIPRE.aOp_mul_one_sub_eq, MIPRE.snorm_aOp_mul_one_sub_le,
  MIPRE.snorm_sq_aOp_mul_one_sub_le, MIPRE.QLD.sum_bornProb_diag_eq, MIPRE.QLD.sum_content_pt,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_uniform_xSqNorm_hatMats_le, MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.hatMats_conjTranspose,
  MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.sum_bornProb_evalMarg_ge, MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.sum_xSqNorm_evalMarg_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.sum_xSqNorm_hat_le, MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.sum_snorm_sq_evalMarg_one_sub_le}
\leanok
Let $(\Phi, S_A, S_B)$ be a simultaneous pair measurement of error $\delta_S$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-simultaneous}) for a strategy of failure $\eps$. For $W \in \{X, Z\}$ and on
average over a uniform $u \in \F_q^m$, with $\widehat S^W_a$ the evaluated $W$ marginal,
\begin{equation*}
  1 - \mathbb E_u \sum_a \bigl\langle \widehat{S}^W_a \ot
    \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u}_{a} \bigr\rangle_\Phi \leq \delta_S ,
\end{equation*}
the opposite party's measurement being the one on the right, and
\begin{equation*}
  \mathbb E_u \sum_a \bigl\| \bigl( \widehat{S}^W_a
    ( \Id - \widehat{M}^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u}_{a} ) \ot \Id \bigr) \Phi \bigr\|^2
  \leq 4 \delta_S + 344\,\eps ,
\end{equation*}
in which \emph{both} factors sit on the same party. All symmetric equivalents hold.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-simultaneous,lem:qld-expanded-points}
\leanok
The first display is the consistency of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-simultaneous} read as an agreement:
for two POVMs with the same outcomes on a unit state, the inconsistency is one minus the
probability that they agree.

For the second, write $T = \widehat S^W_a$ and $A = \widehat M^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u}_a$ on
Alice's register and $B$ for the same point measurement on Bob's; all three are projections, $T$
because the marginal of a projective measurement is projective, $A$ and $B$ because the expanded
point measurements are. Since operators on opposite parties commute and $B^2 = B$,
\[
  (T \ot \Id)(\Id - A \ot \Id)
    = (\Id - \Id \ot B)\,(T \ot \Id - \Id \ot B) - (T \ot \Id)(A \ot \Id - \Id \ot B) ,
\]
an identity whose two right-hand factors in front, $\Id - \Id \ot B$ and $T \ot \Id$, are
contractions. So the same-party deviation is at most the sum of two cross-party ones: the first
is the deviation of the marginal from the opposite party's point measurement, which the first
display bounds by $2\delta_S$ after summing, and the second is the self-consistency of the
expanded point measurements, $172\eps$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-expanded-points}. Squaring costs a
factor two on each. The self-consistency is stated at the verifier's content and used at a
uniform point; the content's $W$ block is uniform, and it transfers to the padded state because
doubly extended operators keep their expanded-state expectations.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} The paper absorbs the $\eps$ of the second item into $\delta_S$; here both
constants are explicit, and the second item's is $4\delta_S + 344\eps$. Both items are stated for
the evaluated marginal at an outcome $a \in \F_q$ rather than per polynomial $g$: the two are the
same sum, grouped differently, and the outcome form is what the Pauli construction consumes.

\effortHard
