From the simultaneous measurement one constructs, for $W \in \{X, Z\}$, measurements and
binary observables on the expanded space which satisfy the Pauli group relations
\emph{exactly} --- linearity in the argument, and $XZ$ commutation or anticommutation with the
phase $(-1)^{\fieldtr(e e' (\tilde{u} \cdot \tilde{v}))}$ at the two combining field elements ---
and which are $\delta$-close to the strategy's $(\mathtt{Point}, W)$ measurements on the expanded
state.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:schwartz-zippel}
\leanok
Exactness of the linearity comes from projectivity of the simultaneous measurement together
with the group law of the Pauli basis; Theorem~\ref{thm:linearity} is \emph{not} used here.
The remaining ingredient bounds the mass the simultaneous measurement places on
non-multilinear outcomes, via Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} applied in the correct
direction --- distinct polynomials of degree $md$ \emph{agree} on at most an $md/q$ fraction,
so it is the agreement and not the disagreement that the bound controls
(Remark~\ref{rem:qld-admitted}).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} Both halves are formalized, for a simultaneous pair measurement
(Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-simultaneous}) of a strategy of failure $\eps$, and both are about the
\emph{same} objects: $\tilde{M}^{W,\ind_m(u)}$ and $\tilde{W}^e(\tilde{u})$ built from that
measurement, on that party's registers. The closeness constant is explicit,
\begin{equation*}
  \delta \;=\; \delta_S + 2\Bigl( \delta_S + \sqrt{688\,\eps} + \frac{md}{q} \Bigr) ,
\end{equation*}
which is the $O(\delta_S + \sqrt{\eps} + md/q)$ the paper absorbs into $\delta_S$. The rest of
this comment records how it was reached, because two of the obstacles it used to list turned out
not to exist.

Of the approximate half's ingredients, three were in place first:
the agreement form of Schwartz--Zippel against every multilinear polynomial at once, the
aggregation that turns it into a bound on the mass at non-multilinear outcomes, and the
substitution error of two families agreeing off that mass
(\texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/NonMultilinear.lean}). A fourth is now too: the strategy's
$(\mathtt{Point}, W)$ measurement and the low-degree reading of its $(\mathtt{Pauli}, W)$ answer
are close \emph{on one party}, at $688\,\eps$
(\texttt{sum\_normSq\_point\_sub\_pauli\_le}), which is what lets the point be sampled
independently of the operators. The two inputs are cross-party items of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}, and they share Alice's point measurement, so one triangle inequality
removes it. A fifth carries such a statement from the strategy's state to the expanded one: every
hatted measurement is a convolution $\widehat X_c = \sum_{a + b = c} X_a \ot T_b$ with the
ancilla's Weyl measurement, and for a \emph{projective} $T$ the cross terms of
$\widehat X^\dagger \widehat X$ vanish, so the common ancilla factor drops out exactly:
$\sum_c \|(\Id \ot \widehat X_c) \widehat\psi\|^2
  = \sum_a \|(\Id \ot X_a) \psi\|^2$ (\texttt{sum\_normSq\_stateVecB\_conv\_eq}). The two
together put the closeness on the expanded state with the same constant
(\texttt{sum\_normSq\_hat\_point\_sub\_pauli\_le}). The aggregation has now been applied to the
simultaneous measurement's outcomes, in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/Multilinear.lean}, and the
bound the appendix's repair turns on is formalized: with $\widehat S^W$ a simultaneous pair
measurement of error $\delta_S$ for a strategy of failure $\eps$,
\begin{equation*}
  \sum_{g \text{ not multilinear}} \bigl\langle \widehat S^W_g \ot \Id \bigr\rangle_\Phi
  \;\leq\; \delta_S + \sqrt{688\,\eps} + \frac{md}{q}
\end{equation*}
(\texttt{SimulPair.sum\_bornProb\_not\_isML\_le}). Its three inputs are the
point-independent family indexed by cube data (\texttt{hatPauli}, the strategy's
$(\mathtt{Pauli}, W)$ measurement convolved with the ancilla's Weyl measurement, projective and
independent of the sampled point, whose reading at a point is the hatted Pauli measurement there
--- \texttt{hatPauli\_map}); the Cauchy--Schwarz that substitutes it for the point measurement at
cost $\sqrt{688\,\eps}$ (\texttt{abs\_sum\_weighted\_bornProb\_le},
\texttt{SimulPair.sum\_bornProb\_hatPauli\_ge}); and Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} per
non-multilinear outcome, uniformly in the cube datum
(\texttt{sum\_uniform\_agree\_ldEnc\_le}). The marginal is taken \emph{indexed by polynomials}
rather than by their value at the point (\texttt{polyMarg}), which is what makes the outcome
operator independent of the point that Schwartz--Zippel then samples.

\emph{And the agreement itself is formalized}: expanding $\tilde{M}^{W,\ind_m(u)}_a$ and
contracting the generalized Pauli against the strategy's point measurement turns the first item's
left-hand side into the outcome sum with the label $\coded(g) \cdot \ind_m(u)$, and
\begin{equation*}
  \mathbb E_u \sum_g \bigl\langle \widehat S^W_g \ot
    \widehat M^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u}_{\coded(g) \cdot \ind_m(u)} \bigr\rangle_\Phi
  \;\geq\; 1 - \delta_S - 2\Bigl( \delta_S + \sqrt{688\,\eps} + \frac{md}{q} \Bigr)
\end{equation*}
(\texttt{SimulPair.sum\_bornProb\_cubeData\_ge}), from the helper's agreement read by polynomial
(\texttt{SimulPair.sum\_bornProb\_polyMarg\_ge}) and the mass bound, through
\texttt{sum\_sub\_le\_of\_eq\_on}.

\emph{One outstanding ingredient turned out not to be needed.} The chain was stated with the good
set ``$g$ is multilinear'', and then agreement of the two labels on it is uniqueness of multilinear
interpolation --- an independent fact about \texttt{MvPolynomial} that
\texttt{sum\_sub\_le\_of\_eq\_on} took as a hypothesis. Taking the good set to be ``$g$
\emph{is} the low-degree encoding of its own cube data'' (\texttt{IsInterp}) removes it: there the
two labels agree by definition (\texttt{dotF\_cubeData\_indVec}), and off it $g$ differs from the
encoding of \emph{every} cube datum (\texttt{ne\_ldEnc\_of\_not\_isInterp}), which is exactly
the hypothesis Schwartz--Zippel wants, uniformly in the datum. Uniqueness of multilinear
interpolation would say the two sets coincide; neither inclusion is used.

\emph{Formalized, unconditionally}, in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/ExactPauli.lean}: the two
marginals of a projective pair measurement (\texttt{sCoarse}, projective by
\texttt{IsPVM.coarse}), the measurement $\tilde{M}^{W,\tilde{u}}_a$ (\texttt{mTilde}) and the
observable $\tilde{W}^e(\tilde{u})$ (\texttt{wTilde}), and the identity everything rests on:
\texttt{wTilde\_eq} says the observable \emph{factorizes} as a signed sum of the pair measurement
tensor a bare generalized Pauli. Self-adjointness, squaring to the identity, and the twisted
commutation relation are then three lines
(\texttt{wTilde\_conjTranspose}, \texttt{wTilde\_mul\_self}, \texttt{wTilde\_mul\_wTilde}), and
linearity in the argument a fourth (\texttt{wTilde\_mul\_add}: both tensor factors are additive,
the sign because the pairing is and the generalized Pauli by the Weyl family's group law), with
\texttt{IsPVM} and \texttt{MIPRE.Weyl.IsWeylFamily} the only hypotheses --- no game, no error term,
and no appeal to Theorem~\ref{thm:linearity}, exactly as the proof above says. All four are
instantiated at a simultaneous pair measurement in \texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/MTilde.lean}
(\texttt{SimulPair.wTildeAt} and its relations), which is what puts them on the same objects as
the closeness below.

\emph{The phase is the general one.} The pinned paper's display for the commutation relation used
to split into $e \neq e'$, claiming exact commutation, and $e = e'$, carrying a phase. That split is
false: self-duality gives $\fieldtr(e_j e_{j'}) = \delta_{j j'}$, but $\fieldtr(e_j e_{j'} c)$ need
not vanish for $c$ outside $\F_2$, and the exponent here is $e e' (\tilde{u} \cdot \tilde{v})$ with
an arbitrary field element in the middle. The paper now displays the general phase, and
\texttt{wTilde\_mul\_wTilde} is that phase: no case split, and no reference to a basis, since the
relation holds for every pair of field elements.

\emph{The critical finding is discharged where it can be}, in
\texttt{MIPRE/Background/QLD/NonMultilinear.lean}.
\texttt{prob\_agree\_ldEnc\_le\_of\_not\_multilinear} is Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} in the
agreement direction and uniformly in the interpolated data, which is what the sum over
$\F_q^M$ needs; \texttt{nonMultilinear\_mass\_le} is the aggregation that turns it into the bound
on the mass the simultaneous measurement places on non-multilinear outcomes; and
\texttt{sum\_sub\_le\_of\_eq\_on} is the substitution error given that mass, taking agreement on
the good set as a hypothesis --- which is why the choice of good set below matters.

\emph{And it is read as a closeness of two measurements}, which is how the paper states item 1.
$\tilde{M}^{W,\ind_m(u)}$ is a projective measurement (\texttt{isPVM\_mTildeAt}, through
\texttt{sTensor\_mul}, so through projectivity of the simultaneous measurement and nothing else),
and
\begin{equation*}
  \mathbb E_u \sum_a \bigl\langle \tilde{M}^{W,\ind_m(u)}_a \ot
    M^{(\mathtt{Point}, W), u}_a \bigr\rangle \;\geq\; 1 - \delta
\end{equation*}
(\texttt{SimulPair.sum\_bornProb\_mTilde\_ge}, and
\texttt{SimulPair.inconsistency\_mTilde\_le} for the $\simeq_\delta$ form). The two are the same
statement grouped differently: expanding $\tilde{M}$ and contracting the generalized Pauli against
the point measurement is \texttt{sum\_kron\_syn\_eq\_hatMats}, one characteristic-two shift.

\emph{The second entangled pair was never needed.} The obstacle recorded here until this point was
that the paper's expanded state $\ket{\hat\psi} = \ket{\psi}_{\alice\bob} \ot
\ket{\EPR}_{\alice'\alice''} \ot \ket{\EPR}_{\bob'\bob''}$ carries \emph{two} entangled
pairs, each local to one party, and is read along two cuts ---
$\alice\alice' \mid \bob\alice''$ for one orientation of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-simultaneous} and
$\bob\bob' \mid \alice\bob''$ for the other --- while \texttt{MIPRE.QLD.hatVec} is one such
cut, with one pair. That is right, and it is all Lemmas~\ref{lem:qld-simultaneous}
and~\ref{lem:qld-helper} need. What it does \emph{not} imply is that
$\tilde{M}^{W,\tilde{u}} = \sum_g \widehat S^W_g \ot \tau^W_{\coded(g) \cdot \tilde{u} -
a}(\tilde{u})$, which wants $\widehat S^W$ and the generalized Pauli on the disjoint registers
$\alice\alice'$ and $\alice''$, needs a new pair: the register $\alice''$ is already there, on
the far side of the cut, and writing $\tilde{M}$ as one matrix is a \emph{regrouping} of the same
six registers along a third cut, $\alice\alice'\alice'' \mid \bob$. Every Born probability
transports across it by \texttt{bornProb\_regroupVec}, which is one entry computation
(\texttt{reindex\_regroupEquiv}) and one invariance of the quadratic form
(\texttt{qform\_comp\_equiv}, the unary companion of \texttt{quadForm\_reindex}, which reindexes
the two parties separately and so cannot move a factor between them). No second pair, no transport
between cuts, and no new estimate.

\emph{A ledger discrepancy worth recording.} Node $1.2.2.15$ declares its dependencies as
$\{1.2.2.13, 1.2.2.14, 1.2.2.7\}$ --- the last being Theorem~\ref{thm:linearity} --- and does not
declare $1.2.2.4$, Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}. The paper's current proof of this step is the other way
round: it does not use exact linearity at all, and it does use the win implications, at
\texttt{enu:qld-pauli-basis-cons} and \texttt{enu:qld-cons}. The \texttt{uses} list
above follows the paper. \texttt{reports/qld-stage5-blueprint-repairs.md} records it.

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[Self-consistency of the exact Pauli observables]
\label{lem:qld-pauli-selfcons}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.15}
\uses{lem:qld-exact-paulis,lem:qld-helper,lem:qld-win,lem:schwartz-zippel,def:cross-distance}
\lean{MIPRE.QLD.syn_mul_syn, MIPRE.QLD.stateVec_expVec_kron_one,
  MIPRE.QLD.stateVecB_expVec_kron_one, MIPRE.QLD.stateVec_hatVec_syn,
  MIPRE.QLD.xSqNorm_hatVec_syn, MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.stateVec_ancSyn,
  MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.sum_ancSyn_mulVec, MIPRE.snorm_sq_sum_orthogonal,
  MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.snorm_sq_evalMarg_eq_sum_polyMarg,
  MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.sum_snorm_sq_polyMarg_one_sub_le, MIPRE.QLD.sum_uniform_agree_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.sum_uniform_agree_mass_le, MIPRE.agreeOp, MIPRE.agreeOp_conjTranspose,
  MIPRE.agreeOp_mul_self, MIPRE.qform_agreeOp, MIPRE.snorm_sq_one_sub_agreeOp,
  MIPRE.snorm_sub_mul_agreeOp_le, MIPRE.sum_kron_le_one, MIPRE.sum_bornProb_kron_le,
  MIPRE.QLD.SimulPair.sum_bornProb_polyMarg_one_sub_le, MIPRE.snorm_sq_proj_mul_eq_qform,
  MIPRE.qform_sandwich_nonneg, MIPRE.snorm_sq_sum_proj_sandwich,
  MIPRE.sum_qform_sandwich_le_of_subset, MIPRE.abs_sum_qform_swap_le,
  MIPRE.sum_snorm_sq_proj_comp_le, MIPRE.snorm_one_sub_aOp_mul_bOp_le,
  MIPRE.sum_snorm_sq_insert_le, MIPRE.sum_xSqNorm_fibre_le}
On average over a \emph{uniform} $\tilde{u} \in \F_q^{M}$, $M = 2^m$, the exact Pauli observable
of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-exact-paulis} on one party agrees with the same observable on the other:
\begin{equation*}
  \bigl( \tilde{W}^e(\tilde{u}) \bigr)_{\alice \alice' \alice''}
  \;\approx_{\delta_{qld}}\;
  \bigl( \tilde{W}^e(\tilde{u}) \bigr)_{\bob \bob' \bob''} .
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\uses{lem:qld-exact-paulis,lem:qld-helper,lem:qld-win,lem:schwartz-zippel}
The paper's ``pulling'' chain. Right-multiplying $\tilde{M}^{W,\tilde{u}}_a$ on one party by the
near-identity $\sum_g (\widehat S^W_g)_{\alice\alice'} \ot
(\widehat M^{(\mathtt{Point},W),u}_{g(u)})_{\bob\alice''}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-helper} and
expanding both definitions rewrites it as a sum over pairs $(g, h)$ with
$(\coded(g) - h) \cdot \tilde{u} = a$ of $(\widehat S^W_g) \ot (\tau^W_h) \ot
(M^{(\mathtt{Point},W),u}_{(g - g_h)(u)})$; the self-consistency of the point measurements
(Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}) then moves the last factor across, and undoing the expansion on the
other side gives the claim. Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} enters where $g$ is compared with
$g_h$, and is where the $md/q$ of $\delta_{qld}$ comes from.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Comments.} \emph{This statement is not in the pinned paper's
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-exact-paulis}; it is the second item of that lemma in the paper, and the
blueprint's paraphrase of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-exact-paulis} dropped it.} That was found by
formalizing: Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-swap}'s item 1 needs the two twirls to be near-invariances of the
state, and the only thing that supplies them is this self-consistency at a \emph{uniform}
$\tilde{u}$ --- not at $\tilde{u} = \ind_m(u)$, which is all
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-exact-paulis} gives. So the dependency graph was missing an edge, and worse, a
node. It is separated out here rather than folded back into
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-exact-paulis} so that the latter's \texttt{leanok} marks stay honest: what is
formalized there is exactly what it states.

\emph{The exact steps of the chain are formalized.} Its eleven displays are of two kinds: steps
marked $\approx_0$, which are identities, and steps marked $\approx_\delta$, which are estimates.
All the identities are in place.

\texttt{syn\_mul\_syn} is display \texttt{eq:qld-pulling-2b}: the syndrome projectors of one Weyl
family at two different probes multiply to the projector onto the outcomes satisfying both
conditions --- off the diagonal the spectral projectors annihilate each other, so only the
eigenvalue patterns in both level sets survive.

\texttt{SimulPair.stateVec\_ancSyn} is \texttt{eq:qld-pulling-3a}: a generalized Pauli moves from
one party's ancilla half to the other's at no cost, the two halves being maximally entangled and
the syndrome projectors symmetric. The point worth recording is that this is available at all.
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-simultaneous} does not say that the padded state \emph{is} an expanded state,
only that it reproduces its expectations, so a \emph{vector} identity about it looks out of reach.
It is not: the identity to be proved is the vanishing of a squared norm, and a squared norm is an
expectation, so \texttt{SimulPair.xSqNorm\_aOp} carries it across with no loss.

\texttt{SimulPair.sum\_ancSyn\_mulVec} is \texttt{eq:qld-pulling-4}: summing the matched pairs of
syndrome projectors, one on each party, leaves the state unchanged, since by the transport each
matched pair acts as the projector on one side alone and those sum to the identity.

\emph{And what the estimates consume is in place.} Two of the $\approx_\delta$ displays,
\texttt{eq:qld-pulling-7} and \texttt{eq:qld-pulling-11}, are the second item of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-helper} --- but not in the indexing that lemma is stated in. The helper is
stated at an outcome $a \in \F_q$, which is what the expansion stage produces, while the chain
sums over the \emph{polynomial} outcomes $g$, because the label $\coded(g) \cdot \tilde{u}$ it
carries is a function of $g$ and not of $g(u)$. The two sums are \emph{equal}, not merely
comparable: within a fibre of the evaluation the pair measurement's outcomes are orthogonal
projectors, so every cross term of the squared norm vanishes
(\texttt{snorm\_sq\_sum\_orthogonal}), and the fibres partition the outcomes. That gives
\texttt{SimulPair.sum\_snorm\_sq\_polyMarg\_one\_sub\_le}, at the helper's own constant
$4\delta_S + 344\,\eps$.

Display \texttt{eq:qld-pulling-12} is Lemma~\ref{lem:schwartz-zippel} again, in the form
\texttt{sum\_uniform\_agree\_mass\_le}: restricting a weighted sum to the tuples where two
distinct outcome polynomials agree at the sampled point costs $md/q$, the weights being Born
probabilities of a projective family and so summing to at most one. No lower bound on $d$ is needed
here, unlike at \texttt{prob\_agree\_ldEnc\_le\_of\_not\_multilinear}: both polynomials carry
the same degree bound, so the hypothesis is already met.

\emph{And the near-identity is a projection.} Display \texttt{eq:qld-pulling-1} right-multiplies
by $\sum_g (\widehat S^W_g)_{\alice\alice'} \ot
(\widehat M^{(\mathtt{Point},W),u}_{g(u)})_{\bob\alice''}$, which the first item of
Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-helper} says is close to the identity on the state. What makes the step cheap
is that this operator is a \emph{projection} (\texttt{agreeOp\_mul\_self}): the pair
measurement's outcomes are orthogonal, so the cross terms vanish, and the opposite party's factors
are projectors. Its deficit from the identity is then exactly one minus the agreement
(\texttt{snorm\_sq\_one\_sub\_agreeOp}) --- linear in the operator, with no Cauchy--Schwarz
anywhere --- and a contraction on the left cannot amplify it
(\texttt{snorm\_sub\_mul\_agreeOp\_le}). The package asks only the \emph{first} party's family
to be projective, which is what lets it apply here, where the second party's factors are indexed
by $g$ through $g(u)$ and so are not a measurement in $g$.

\emph{And the two ends of \texttt{eq:qld-pulling-10}'s justification.} That display is argued by
bounding the magnitude of a difference, and the argument runs from
\texttt{fact:add-a-proj} at the top to the helper at the bottom. Both ends are in place. The top
is \texttt{sum\_kron\_le\_one}: a family of projectors on one factor, tensored with a projective
measurement on another, sums to at most the identity, because the complement
$\sum_x (\Id - B_x) \ot T_x$ is a sum of positive semidefinite terms; against a positive
operator on the other party that discards the index the sandwich runs over
(\texttt{sum\_bornProb\_kron\_le}). The bottom is \texttt{eq:qld-pulling-14},
\texttt{SimulPair.sum\_bornProb\_polyMarg\_one\_sub\_le}: the complement of the helper's
agreement, read by polynomial outcome, is at most $\delta_S$, the marginal's outcomes summing to
the identity.

\emph{And the middle of that justification, which is the only Cauchy--Schwarz in the chain.}
The estimate expands a squared norm over the orthogonal outcomes of a projective family into a sum
of sandwiches, one per outcome (\texttt{snorm\_sq\_sum\_proj\_sandwich}); drops the constraint
on the outcomes, every sandwich being nonnegative
(\texttt{sum\_qform\_sandwich\_le\_of\_subset}); and then \emph{moves} the sandwiched
measurement from one party to the other. That is \texttt{abs\_sum\_qform\_swap\_le}, display
\texttt{eq:qld-pulling-13}: the difference of the two sandwiches is two terms each carrying the
deviation on one side, and each is bounded by $\sqrt{\eps}$ against a mass at most one, so the
substitution costs $2\sqrt{\eps}$. The side that is left as a single factor rather than a
sandwich is the one whose measurement commutes with what it sandwiches, where $XSX = XS$.

\emph{And \texttt{eq:qld-pulling-3}.} The expansion leaves one party's projector beside the
\emph{other} party's point measurement, and the chain inserts a copy of that measurement on the
first party's side, spending the self-consistency of Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-win}. Two things make the
step cost that and no more. The deficit of the insertion is the cross-party deviation itself
(\texttt{snorm\_one\_sub\_aOp\_mul\_bOp\_le}): $(\Id - A)B = B(B - A)$, the outcome inserted
beside being a projection and the parties commuting, and a projection in front costs nothing. And
the family in front is indexed \emph{through a map} to the measurement's own outcomes, so the sum
sees only the fibres of that map (\texttt{sum\_snorm\_sq\_proj\_comp\_le}). Without the fibres
the step would be false: the family's index is far larger than the outcome set, and paying one
deviation per index would multiply the bound by the size of a fibre. The display is
\texttt{sum\_snorm\_sq\_insert\_le}.

\emph{And the final passage was already in the library.} Coarse-graining two projective
measurements the same way costs nothing --- which is Item 2 of \texttt{fact:agreement}, then
\texttt{fact:data-processing}, then Item 1 of \texttt{fact:agreement} --- and Foundations has it
as \texttt{sum\_xSqNorm\_map\_le}, on bundled POVMs. The chain speaks \texttt{IsPVM};
\texttt{IsPVM.toPOVM} and \texttt{sum\_xSqNorm\_fibre\_le} bridge the two.

\emph{Not formalized}: the assembly of the chain itself --- the index bookkeeping of its eleven
displays, and Lemma~\ref{lem:qld-povm-to-obs} at the end. Every step it assembles is in place.
\texttt{reports/qld-stage5-blueprint-repairs.md} records the omission that created this node.

\effortHard

\begin{lemma}[The swap isometry]
\label{lem:qld-swap}
\ledgernode{1.2.2.16}
\uses{lem:qld-exact-paulis,lem:qld-pauli-selfcons,lem:qld-win,lem:schwartz-zippel,
